     8→
     9→# Edit Module
    10→
    11→**Goal:** Modify existing BMAD module briefs or module structures while maintaining coherence and compliance.
    12→
    13→**Your Role:** You are the **Module Architect** — a specialist in BMAD module design and maintenance. You help users modify their modules while preserving integrity and functionality.
    14→
    15→---
    16→
    17→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    18→
    19→This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution.
    20→
    21→### Core Principles
    22→
    23→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file
    24→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory
    25→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order
    26→- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter
    27→- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed
    28→
    29→### Step Processing Rules
    30→
    31→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
    32→2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order
    33→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
    34→4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue, only proceed when user selects 'C'
    35→5. **SAVE STATE**: Update frontmatter before loading next step
    36→6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
    37→
    38→### Critical Rules
    39→
    40→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    41→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    42→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
    43→- 💾 **ALWAYS** update frontmatter when writing final output for a step
    44→- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow exact instructions in step files
    45→- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for input
    46→- 📋 **NEVER** create mental todo lists from future steps
    47→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with config `{communication_language}`
    48→
    49→---
    50→
    51→## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE

    53→### 1. Configuration Loading

    55→Load and read full config from `{project-root}/_bmad/bmb/config.yaml` and resolve:

     1→---
     2→title: Getting Started
     3→description: Installation, prerequisites, first steps, and common use cases for Skill Forge
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Getting Started with SKF: Skill Forge
     7→
     8→Welcome to Skill Forge! This guide will help you get up and running.
     9→
    10→---
    11→
    12→## What This Module Does
    13→
    14→Skill Forge is an automated skill compiler for the AI agent ecosystem. It transforms code repositories, documentation websites, and developer discourse into agentskills.io-compliant, version-pinned, provenance-backed agent skills. Every instruction traces to actual code — zero hallucination tolerance.
    15→
    16→---
    17→
    18→## Installation
    19→
    20→There are three ways to install SKF, depending on your setup.
    21→
    22→### Standalone (recommended for trying SKF)
    23→
    24→```bash
    25→npx bmad-module-skill-forge install
    26→```
    27→
    28→Installs SKF on its own. You'll be prompted for project name, output folders, and which IDEs to configure. The installer generates IDE-specific command files (e.g. `.claude/commands/`, `.cursor/commands/`) so workflows appear in your IDE's command palette.
    29→
    30→### As a custom module during BMad Method installation
    31→
    32→```bash
    33→npx bmad-method install
    34→```
    35→
    36→When prompted **"Add custom modules from your computer?"**, select Yes and provide the path to the SKF `src/` folder (clone this repo first):
    37→
    38→```
    39→Path to custom module folder: /path/to/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/
    40→```
    41→
    42→This installs BMad core + SKF together with full IDE integration, manifests, and help catalog. Best when you want the complete BMad development workflow.
    43→
    44→### Add SKF to an existing BMad project
    45→
    46→If you already have BMad installed, you can add SKF afterward by running the standalone installer in the same directory:
    47→
    48→```bash
    49→npx bmad-module-skill-forge install
    50→```
    51→
    52→The installer detects the existing `_bmad/` directory and installs SKF alongside your current modules.
    53→
    54→---
    55→
    56→## Prerequisites
    57→
    58→| Tool                                                                   | Required For       | Install                       |
    59→|------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------|-----------------------------|
    60→| [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) >= 22                                   | Installation, npx commands | <https://nodejs.org>          |
    61→| `gh` (GitHub CLI)                                                      | All modes          | <https://cli.github.com>      |
    62→| `ast-grep`  (CLI tool for code structural search, lint, and rewriting) | Forge + Deep modes | <https://ast-grep.github.io>  |
    63→| `qmd` (local hybrid search engine for project files)                   | Deep mode          | <https://github.com/tobi/qmd> |
    64→
    65→Don't worry if you don't have all tools — SKF detects what's available and sets your tier automatically.
    66→
    67→---
    68→
    69→## First Steps
    70→
    71→### 1. Setup Your Forge
    72→
    73→```
    74→@Ferris SF
    75→```
    76→
    77→This detects your tools, sets your capability tier, and initializes the forge environment. You only need to do this once per project.
    78→
    79→### 2. Generate Your First Skill
    80→
    81→**Fastest path (Quick Skill):**
    82→```
    83→@Ferris QS https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD
    84→```
    85→
    86→Ferris reads the repository, extracts the public API, and generates a skill in under a minute.
    87→
    88→**Full quality path:**
    89→```
    90→@Ferris BS    # Brief — scope and design the skill
    91→@Ferris CS    # Create — compile from the brief
    92→@Ferris TS    # Test — verify completeness
    93→@Ferris EX    # Export — package for distribution
    94→```
    95→
    96→### 3. Stack Skill (for full projects)
    97→
    98→```
    99→@Ferris SS
   100→```
   101→
   102→Analyzes your project's dependencies and generates a consolidated stack skill with integration patterns.
   103→
   104→---
   105→
   106→## Common Use Cases
   107→
   108→### My agent keeps hallucinating API calls
   109→
   110→Your agent invents function signatures that don't exist. Generate a verified skill so it works from structural truth instead of guessing.
   111→
   112→```
   113→@Ferris QS https://github.com/org/library
   114→```
   115→
   116→The skill pins every function to its actual source location. Hallucinations stop.
   117→
   118→### I'm adopting a new library and need my agent to use it correctly
   119→
   120→You added a dependency but your agent doesn't know its API yet. Quick Skill resolves package names across npm, PyPI, and crates.io.
   121→
   122→```
   123→@Ferris QS cognee
   124→```
   125→
   126→Ferris resolves the package to its GitHub repo, extracts the public API, and generates a skill your agent can reference immediately.
   127→
   128→### I want my agent to understand my entire project stack
   129→
   130→Individual skills cover single libraries. Stack Skill maps how your dependencies interact — shared types, co-import patterns, integration points.
   131→
   132→```
   133→@Ferris SS
   134→```
   135→
   136→Ferris detects your manifests, ranks dependencies by significance, and generates a consolidated skill with cross-library integration patterns.
   137→
   138→### I'm onboarding a large existing codebase
   139→
   140→A brownfield repo with dozens of modules. You need to know what's worth skilling before you start.
   141→
   142→```
   143→@Ferris AN
   144→```
   145→
   146→Analyze Source scans the project, identifies skillable units, maps exports, and generates recommended briefs you can batch-create with `@Ferris CS --batch`.
   147→
   148→### I want the highest accuracy possible
   149→
   150→Quick mode reads source files. Forge mode adds AST parsing for structural truth. Deep mode goes further — as skills are compiled, SKF indexes curated extraction artifacts and skill briefs into QMD collections, then uses BM25 keyword search, vector semantic search, and LLM-powered re-ranking to surface context no other tool can find.
   151→
   152→```
   153→@Ferris SF    # Setup detects your tools and sets tier automatically
   154→```
   155→
   156→If `ast-grep` and `qmd` are available, Ferris activates Deep mode. Every skill gets AST-verified signatures (T1) enriched with deep knowledge search (T2) — deprecation warnings, breaking change history, common pitfalls surfaced by searching across your indexed skill artifacts.
   157→
   158→### I maintain an OSS library and want official skills
   159→
   160→You want maximum quality with full provenance for distribution. Use the full pipeline with Deep mode for the richest output.
   161→
   162→```
   163→@Ferris BS    # Scope and design the skill
   164→@Ferris CS    # Compile — AST extraction + QMD enrichment (Deep)
   165→@Ferris TS    # Verify completeness before publishing
   166→@Ferris EX    # Package for distribution
   167→```
   168→
   169→Export with `source_authority: official`. Consumers get verified skills that update with each release.
   170→
   171→### A dependency shipped breaking changes
   172→
   173→Your existing skill is now out of date. Audit detects the drift, Update regenerates while preserving your manual additions.
   174→
   175→```
   176→@Ferris AS    # Find what changed
   177→@Ferris US    # Regenerate — [MANUAL] sections survive
   178→@Ferris TS    # Verify the update
   179→```
   180→
   181→---
   182→
   183→## What's Next?
   184→
   185→- Check out the [Agents Reference](../agents.md) to learn about Ferris
   186→- Browse the [Workflows Reference](../workflows.md) to see all available commands
   187→- See [Examples](../examples.md) for real-world usage scenarios
   188→
   189→---
   190→
   191→## Need Help?
   192→
   193→If you run into issues:
   194→1. Run `/bmad-help` — analyzes your current state and suggests what to do next
   195→   (e.g. `/bmad-help my quick skill has low confidence scores, how do I improve them?`)
   196→   *Provided by the [BMad Method](https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD) — not available in standalone SKF installations.*
   197→2. Run `@Ferris SF` to check your tool availability and tier
   198→3. Check `forge-config.yaml` for your current configuration
   199→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Workflows
     3→description: All 10 SKF workflows with commands, steps, and connection diagram
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Workflows Reference
     7→
     8→SKF includes 10 workflows organized by purpose:
     9→
    10→---
    11→
    12→## Core Workflows
    13→
    14→### Setup Forge (SF)
    15→
    16→**Command:** `@Ferris SF`
    17→
    18→**Purpose:** Initialize forge environment, detect tools, set capability tier, verify QMD collection health.
    19→
    20→**When to Use:** First time using SKF in a project. Run once per project.
    21→
    22→**Key Steps:** Detect tools → Determine tier → Create forge-config.yaml → QMD hygiene (Deep)
    23→
    24→**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
    25→
    26→---
    27→
    28→### Brief Skill (BS)
    29→
    30→**Command:** `@Ferris BS`
    31→
    32→**Purpose:** Scope and design a skill through guided discovery.
    33→
    34→**When to Use:** Before `Create Skill` when you want maximum control over what gets compiled.
    35→
    36→**Key Steps:** Gather intent → Analyze target → Define scope → Write skill-brief.yaml
    37→
    38→**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
    39→
    40→---
    41→
    42→### Create Skill (CS)
    43→
    44→**Command:** `@Ferris CS`
    45→
    46→**Purpose:** Compile a skill from a brief. Supports `--batch` for multiple briefs.
    47→
    48→**When to Use:** After Brief Skill, or with an existing skill-brief.yaml.
    49→
    50→**Key Steps:** Load brief → Ecosystem check → AST extract → QMD enrich → Compile → Validate → Generate
    51→
    52→**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
    53→
    54→---
    55→
    56→### Update Skill (US)
    57→
    58→**Command:** `@Ferris US`
    59→
    60→**Purpose:** Smart regeneration preserving `[MANUAL]` sections. Detects individual vs stack internally.
    61→
    62→**When to Use:** After source code changes when an existing skill needs updating.
    63→
    64→**Key Steps:** Load existing → Detect changes → Re-extract → Merge (preserve MANUAL) → Validate → Write
    65→
    66→**Agent:** Ferris (Surgeon mode)
    67→
    68→---
    69→
    70→## Feature Workflows
    71→
    72→### Quick Skill (QS)
    73→
    74→**Command:** `@Ferris QS <package-or-url>`
    75→
    76→**Purpose:** Brief-less fast skill with package-to-repo resolution.
    77→
    78→**When to Use:** When you need a skill quickly — no brief needed. Accepts package names or GitHub URLs.
    79→
    80→**Key Steps:** Resolve target → Ecosystem check → Quick extract → Compile → Validate → Write
    81→
    82→**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
    83→
    84→---
    85→
    86→### Stack Skill (SS)
    87→
    88→**Command:** `@Ferris SS`
    89→
    90→**Purpose:** Consolidated project stack skill with integration patterns.
    91→
    92→**When to Use:** When you want your agent to understand your entire project stack — not just individual libraries.
    93→
    94→**Key Steps:** Detect manifests → Rank dependencies → Scope confirmation → Parallel extract → Detect integrations → Compile stack → Generate references
    95→
    96→**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
    97→
    98→---
    99→
   100→### Analyze Source (AN)
   101→
   102→**Command:** `@Ferris AN`
   103→
   104→**Purpose:** Decomposition engine — discover what to skill, recommend stack skill.
   105→
   106→**When to Use:** Brownfield onboarding of large repos or multi-service projects.
   107→
   108→**Key Steps:** Scan project → Identify units → Map exports → Detect integrations → Recommend → Generate briefs
   109→
   110→**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
   111→
   112→---
   113→
   114→### Audit Skill (AS)
   115→
   116→**Command:** `@Ferris AS`
   117→
   118→**Purpose:** Drift detection between skill and current source.
   119→
   120→**When to Use:** To check if a skill has fallen out of date with its source code.
   121→
   122→**Key Steps:** Load skill → Re-index source → Structural diff → Semantic diff (Deep) → Classify severity → Report
   123→
   124→**Agent:** Ferris (Audit mode)
   125→
   126→---
   127→
   128→### Test Skill (TS)
   129→
   130→**Command:** `@Ferris TS`
   131→
   132→**Purpose:** Cognitive completeness verification. Quality gate before export.
   133→
   134→**When to Use:** After creating or updating a skill, before exporting.
   135→
   136→**Key Steps:** Load skill → Detect mode → Coverage check → Coherence check → Score → Gap report
   137→
   138→**Agent:** Ferris (Audit mode)
   139→
   140→---
   141→
   142→## Utility Workflows
   143→
   144→### Export Skill (EX)
   145→
   146→**Command:** `@Ferris EX`
   147→
   148→**Purpose:** Package for distribution + platform-aware context injection into CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md.
   149→
   150→**When to Use:** When a skill is ready for distribution or CLAUDE.md integration.
   151→
   152→**Key Steps:** Load skill → Package → Generate snippet → Update CLAUDE.md → Token report → Summary
   153→
   154→**Agent:** Ferris (Delivery mode)
   155→
   156→---
   157→
   158→## Workflow Connections
   159→
   160→```
   161→                    setup-forge (one-time)
   162→                         │
   163→          ┌──────────────┼──────────────┐
   164→          │              │              │
   165→     analyze-source  quick-skill  create-stack-skill
   166→          │              │              │
   167→     brief-skill         │              │
   168→          │              │              │
   169→     create-skill        │              │
   170→          │              │              │
   171→          └──────┬───────┘──────────────┘
   172→                 │
   173→            test-skill (quality gate)
   174→                 │
   175→          ┌──────┼──────┐
   176→          │             │
   177→     export-skill  audit-skill
   178→                        │
   179→                   update-skill (loops back to test)
   180→```
   181→
   182→---
   183→
   184→## Build Priority
   185→
   186→| Phase | Workflows | Notes |
   187→|-------|-----------|-------|
   188→| 1a (Core Engine) | setup-forge, create-skill, test-skill, export-skill | Must build first |
   189→| 1b (Entry Points) | quick-skill, brief-skill, create-stack-skill | Complete v1.0 |
   190→| 2 (Lifecycle) | audit-skill, update-skill, analyze-source | Post-launch |
   191→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Architecture
     3→description: How Skill Forge works — output format, confidence model, progressive tiers, tool ecosystem, and key design decisions
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Architecture
     7→
     8→This page explains how SKF works under the hood — the output format, confidence model, progressive capability tiers, tool ecosystem, and the design decisions that make every instruction traceable.
     9→
    10→---
    11→
    12→## Why Another Tool?
    13→
    14→AI agents hallucinate APIs. Not sometimes — constantly. The table below shows why every existing approach fails at scale:
    15→
    16→| Approach | Strength | Fatal Flaw |
    17→|----------|----------|------------|
    18→| `npx skills init` | Format compliant | Empty shell. 0% intelligence. |
    19→| LLM Summarization | High semantic context | Hallucination. Guesses parameters. No grounding. |
    20→| RAG / Context stuffing | Good retrieval | Fragmented. Finds snippets, fails to synthesize. |
    21→| Manual Authoring | High initial quality | Drift. Doesn't scale. |
    22→| Copilot/Cursor built-in | Convenient | Generic. Doesn't know YOUR integration patterns. |
    23→| **Skill Forge** | **Structural truth + automation** | **Rigid. (Feature, not bug.)** |
    24→
    25→SKF solves this by mechanically extracting function signatures, type definitions, and usage patterns from source code — then compiling them into verifiable, version-pinned skills that comply with the [agentskills.io specification](https://agentskills.io/specification).
    26→
    27→---
    28→
    29→## Progressive Capability Model
    30→
    31→SKF uses an additive tier model. Each tier is the previous tier plus one tool. You never lose capability by adding a tool.
    32→
    33→| Tier | Tools | What You Get |
    34→|------|-------|-------------|
    35→| **Quick** | `gh_bridge` + `skill-check` + `tessl` | Source reading + spec validation + content quality review. Best-effort skills in under a minute. |
    36→| **Forge** | + `ast_bridge` | Structural truth. AST-verified signatures. Co-import detection. T1 confidence. |
    37→| **Deep** | + `qmd_bridge` | Knowledge search. Temporal provenance. Drift detection. Full intelligence. |
    38→
    39→Setup detects your installed tools and sets your tier automatically:
    40→
    41→```
    42→@Ferris SF
    43→```
    44→
    45→```
    46→Forge initialized. Tools: gh, ast-grep, QMD. Tier: Deep. Ready.
    47→```
    48→
    49→Don't have ast-grep or QMD yet? No problem — Quick mode works with just the GitHub CLI. Install tools later; your tier upgrades automatically.
    50→
    51→### Tier Override — Comparing Output Across Tiers
    52→
    53→You can force a specific tier by setting `tier_override` in your preferences file (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/preferences.yaml`):
    54→
    55→```yaml
    56→# Force Forge tier regardless of detected tools
    57→tier_override: Forge
    58→```
    59→
    60→This is useful for comparing skill quality across tiers for the same target:
    61→
    62→```
    63→# 1. Set tier_override: Quick in preferences.yaml
    64→@Ferris CS                # compile at Quick tier
    65→
    66→# 2. Change to tier_override: Forge
    67→@Ferris CS                # recompile at Forge tier — compare output
    68→
    69→# 3. Reset to tier_override: ~ (auto-detect)
    70→```
    71→
    72→Set `tier_override` to `Quick`, `Forge`, or `Deep`. Set to `~` (null) to return to auto-detection. The override is respected by all tier-aware workflows (CS, SS, US, AS, TS).
    73→
    74→---
    75→
    76→## Confidence Tiers
    77→
    78→Every claim in a generated skill carries a confidence tier that traces to its source:
    79→
    80→| Tier | Source | Tool | What It Means |
    81→|------|--------|------|---------------|
    82→| **T1** | AST extraction | `ast_bridge` | Current code, structurally verified. Immutable for that version. |
    83→| **T2** | QMD evidence / source reading | `qmd_bridge` / `gh_bridge` | Historical + planned context (issues, PRs, changelogs, docs). |
    84→| **T3** | External documentation | `doc_fetcher` | External, untrusted. Quarantined. |
    85→
    86→### Temporal Provenance
    87→
    88→Confidence tiers map to temporal scopes:
    89→
    90→- **T1-now (instructions):** What ast-grep sees in the checked-out code. This is what your agent executes.
    91→- **T2-past (annotations):** Closed issues, merged PRs, changelogs — why the API looks the way it does.
    92→- **T2-future (annotations):** Open PRs, deprecation warnings, RFCs — what's coming.
    93→
    94→Progressive disclosure controls how much context surfaces at each level:
    95→
    96→| Output | Content |
    97→|--------|---------|
    98→| `context-snippet.md` | T1-now only — compressed, always-on |
    99→| `SKILL.md` | T1-now + lightweight T2 annotations |
   100→| `references/` | Full temporal context with all tiers |
   101→
   102→### Tier Constrains Authority
   103→
   104→Your forge tier limits what authority claims a skill can make:
   105→
   106→| Forge Tier | AST? | QMD? | Max Authority | Accuracy Guarantee |
   107→|-----------|------|------|---------------|-------------------|
   108→| Quick | No | No | `community` | Best-effort |
   109→| Forge | Yes | No | `official` | Structural (AST-verified) |
   110→| Deep | Yes | Yes | `official` | Full (structural + contextual + temporal) |
   111→
   112→---
   113→
   114→## Output Architecture
   115→
   116→### Per-Skill Output
   117→
   118→Every generated skill produces a self-contained directory:
   119→
   120→```
   121→skills/{name}/
   122→├── SKILL.md              # Active skill (loaded on trigger)
   123→├── context-snippet.md    # Passive context (compressed, always-on)
   124→├── metadata.json         # Machine-readable provenance
   125→└── references/           # Progressive disclosure
   126→    ├── {function-a}.md
   127→    ├── {function-b}.md
   128→    └── integrations/     # Stack skills only
   129→        ├── auth-db.md
   130→        └── pwa-auth.md
   131→```
   132→
   133→### SKILL.md Format
   134→
   135→Skills follow the [agentskills.io specification](https://agentskills.io/specification) with frontmatter:
   136→
   137→```yaml
   138→---
   139→name: payment-service
   140→version: 2.1.0
   141→description: Payment processing API skill — 23 verified functions
   142→author: org/payment-team
   143→---
   144→```
   145→
   146→Every instruction in the body traces to source:
   147→
   148→```
   149→Extracted: `getToken(userId: string, options?: TokenOptions): Promise<AuthToken>`
   150→[AST:src/auth/index.ts:L42]. Confidence: T1.
   151→```
   152→
   153→### metadata.json — The Birth Certificate
   154→
   155→Machine-readable provenance for every skill:
   156→
   157→```json
   158→{
   159→  "name": "payment-service",
   160→  "version": "2.1.0",
   161→  "skill_type": "individual",
   162→  "source_authority": "official",
   163→  "source_repo": "github.com/org/payment-service",
   164→  "source_commit": "a1b2c3d",
   165→  "forge_tier": "forge",
   166→  "spec_version": "1.3",
   167→  "generated_at": "2026-02-25T14:30:00Z",
   168→  "stats": {
   169→    "exports_documented": 23,
   170→    "exports_total": 23,
   171→    "coverage": 1.0,
   172→    "confidence_t1": 20,
   173→    "confidence_t2": 3,
   174→    "confidence_t3": 0
   175→  }
   176→}
   177→```
   178→
   179→### Stack Skill Output
   180→
   181→Stack skills map how your dependencies interact — shared types, co-import patterns, integration points:
   182→
   183→```
   184→skills/{project}-stack/
   185→├── SKILL.md              # Integration patterns + project conventions
   186→├── context-snippet.md    # Compressed stack index
   187→├── metadata.json         # Component versions, integration graph
   188→└── references/
   189→    ├── nextjs.md         # Project-specific subset
   190→    ├── better-auth.md    # Project-specific subset
   191→    └── integrations/
   192→        ├── auth-db.md    # Cross-library pattern
   193→        └── pwa-auth.md   # Cross-library pattern
   194→```
   195→
   196→The primary source is your project repo. Component references trace to library repos. `skill_type: "stack"` in metadata.
   197→
   198→---
   199→
   200→## Dual-Output Strategy
   201→
   202→Based on [Vercel research](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills): passive context (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md) achieves 100% pass rate vs 53% for active skills alone.
   203→
   204→Every skill generates both:
   205→
   206→1. **SKILL.md** — Active skill loaded on trigger with full instructions
   207→2. **context-snippet.md** — Passive context, compressed index injected into CLAUDE.md
   208→
   209→### Managed CLAUDE.md Section
   210→
   211→Export injects a managed section between markers:
   212→
   213→```markdown
   214→<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:2026-02-25 -->
   215→[SKF Skills]|3 skills|1 stack
   216→|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
   217→|
   218→|payment-service → skills/payment-service/
   219→|  exports: getToken, refreshToken, revokeSession, createSession
   220→|
   221→|auth-service → skills/auth-service/
   222→|  exports: getSession, validateToken, revokeSession, createUser
   223→|
   224→|my-project-stack → skills/my-project-stack/
   225→|  stack: next@15, better-auth@3, spacetimedb@1, serwist@9
   226→|  integrations: auth↔db, pwa↔auth, gateway↔auth
   227→<!-- SKF:END -->
   228→```
   229→
   230→Two lines per skill (~30 tokens each). Developer controls placement. Ferris controls content. Snippet updates only happen at `export-skill` — create and update are draft operations.
   231→
   232→---
   233→
   234→## Tool Ecosystem
   235→
   236→### 4 MCP Tools
   237→
   238→| Tool | Wraps | Purpose |
   239→|------|-------|---------|
   240→| **`gh_bridge`** | GitHub CLI (`gh`) | Source code access, issue mining, release tracking, PR intelligence |
   241→| **`skill-check`** | [thedaviddias/skill-check](https://github.com/thedaviddias/skill-check) | Validation + auto-fix (`check --fix`), quality scoring (0-100), security scan, split-body, diff comparison |
   242→| **`tessl`** | [tessl](https://tessl.io) | Content quality review, actionability scoring, progressive disclosure evaluation, AI judge with suggestions |
   243→| **`ast_bridge`** | ast-grep CLI | Structural extraction, custom AST queries, co-import detection |
   244→| **`qmd_bridge`** | QMD (local search) | BM25 keyword search, vector semantic search, collection indexing |
   245→
   246→Optional addon: **`doc_fetcher`** for remote documentation (Firecrawl/Jina.ai). Output quarantined as T3.
   247→
   248→### Conflict Resolution
   249→
   250→When tools disagree, higher priority wins for instructions. Lower priority is preserved as annotations:
   251→
   252→| Priority | Source | Tool |
   253→|----------|--------|------|
   254→| 1 (highest) | AST extraction | `ast_bridge` |
   255→| 2 | QMD evidence | `qmd_bridge` |
   256→| 3 | Source reading (non-AST) | `gh_bridge` |
   257→| 4 | External documentation | `doc_fetcher` |
   258→
   259→### Internal Utility
   260→
   261→**`manifest_reader`** detects and parses dependency files across ecosystems:
   262→
   263→- **Full support:** `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`
   264→- **Basic support:** `build.gradle`, `pom.xml`, `Gemfile`, `composer.json`
   265→
   266→---
   267→
   268→## Workspace Artifacts
   269→
   270→Build artifacts are committable — another developer can reproduce the same skill:
   271→
   272→```
   273→forge-data/{skill-name}/
   274→├── skill-brief.yaml        # Compilation config
   275→├── provenance-map.json     # Source map with AST bindings
   276→├── evidence-report.md      # Build audit trail
   277→└── extraction-rules.yaml   # Language-specific ast-grep schema
   278→```
   279→
   280→`skills/` and `forge-data/` are committed. Agent memory (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/`) is gitignored.
   281→
   282→---
   283→
   284→## Ownership Model
   285→
   286→| Context | `source_authority` | Distribution |
   287→|---------|-------------------|-------------|
   288→| OSS library (maintainer generates) | `official` | `npx skills publish` to agentskills ecosystem |
   289→| Internal service (team generates) | `internal` | `skills/` in repo, ships with code |
   290→| External dependency (consumer generates) | `community` | Local `skills/`, marked as community |
   291→
   292→Provenance maps enable verification: an `official` skill's provenance must trace to the actual source repo owned by the author.
   293→
   294→---
   295→
   296→## Key Design Decisions
   297→
   298→| Decision | Rationale |
   299→|----------|-----------|
   300→| **Solo agent (Ferris), not multi-agent** | One domain (skill compilation) doesn't benefit from handoffs. Shared knowledge base (AST patterns, provenance maps) is the core asset. |
   301→| **Workflows drive modes, not conversation** | Ferris doesn't auto-switch based on question content. Invoke a workflow to change mode. Predictable behavior. |
   302→| **Hub-and-spoke cross-knowledge** | Each skill has one primary source. Cross-repo references use inline summary + pointer + `[XREF:repo:file:line]` provenance tag. |
   303→| **Stack skill = compositional** | SKILL.md is the integration layer. references/ contains per-library + integration pairs. Partial regeneration on dependency updates. |
   304→| **Snippet updates only at export** | Create/update are draft operations. Export publishes to skills/ and CLAUDE.md. No half-baked snippets. |
   305→| **Bundle spec with opt-in update** | Offline-capable. 90-day staleness warning. `setup-forge --update-spec` fetches latest. |
   306→
   307→---
   308→
   309→## Security
   310→
   311→- All tool wrappers use array-style subprocess execution — no shell interpolation
   312→- Input sanitization: allowlist characters for repo names, file paths, patterns
   313→- File paths validated against project root (no directory traversal)
   314→- **Source code never leaves the machine.** All processing is local (AST, QMD, validation).
   315→- `doc_fetcher` warns before transmitting URLs to external services
   316→
   317→---
   318→
   319→## Ecosystem Alignment
   320→
   321→SKF produces skills compatible with the [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io) ecosystem:
   322→
   323→- Full [specification](https://agentskills.io/specification) compliance
   324→- Distribution via [`npx skills add/publish`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/skills)
   325→- Compatible with [agentskills/agentskills](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills) and [vercel-labs/skills](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills)
   326→
```

    24→### Brownfield Platform — 8 Minutes

    30→@Ferris AN          # Analyze — 10 services mapped
    31→@Ferris CS --batch  # Create — batch generation

    35→
    36→### Release Prep — Trust Builder
    37→
    38→Sarah prepares v3.0.0 with breaking changes.

    40→```
    41→@Ferris AS    # Audit — finds 3 renames, 1 removal, 1 addition
    42→@Ferris US    # Update — preserves [MANUAL] sections, adds annotations
    43→@Ferris TS    # Test — verify completeness
    44→@Ferris EX    # Export — package for npm release
    45→```
    46→
    47→Ships with npm release. Consumers upgrade — their agents use the correct function names. Zero hallucination tickets.
    48→
    49→### Stack Skill — Integration Intelligence
    50→
    51→Armel's full-stack project: Next.js + Serwist + SpacetimeDB + better-auth.

    53→```
    54→@Ferris SS
    55→```
    56→
    57→Ferris detects 8 significant dependencies, finds 5 co-import integration points. Generates a consolidated stack skill. The agent now knows: "When you modify the auth flow, update the Serwist cache exclusion at `src/sw.ts:L23`." Integration intelligence no other tool provides.
    58→
    59→---
    60→
    61→## Common Scenarios
    62→
    63→### Scenario A: Greenfield + BMM Integration
    64→
    65→BMAD user starts a new project. BMM architect suggests skill generation after retrospective.
    66→
    67→```
    68→@Ferris BS    # Brief — scope the skill
    69→@Ferris CS    # Create — compile from brief
    70→@Ferris TS    # Test — verify completeness
    71→@Ferris EX    # Export — inject into CLAUDE.md
    72→```
    73→
    74→Skills accumulate over sprints. Agent gets smarter every iteration.
    75→
    76→### Scenario B: Multi-Repo Platform
    77→
    78→Alex needs cross-service knowledge for 10 microservices.
    79→
    80→One forge project, multiple QMD collections, hub-and-spoke skills with integration patterns.
    81→
    82→### Scenario C: External Dependency
    83→
    84→Developer needs skills for a library that doesn't have official skills.
    85→
    86→```
    87→@Ferris QS better-auth
    88→```
    89→
    90→Checks ecosystem first. If no official skill exists: generates from source. `source_authority: community`.
    91→
    92→### Scenario D: Docs-Only (SaaS/Closed Source)
    93→
    94→No source code available — only documentation.
    95→
    96→Generate from docs + QMD-indexed content. T2/T3 confidence only. `source_authority: community`.
    97→
    98→---
    99→
   100→## Tips & Tricks
   101→
   102→### Progressive Capability
   103→
   104→Start with Quick mode (no setup required), upgrade to Forge (install ast-grep), then Deep (install QMD). Each tier builds on the previous — you never lose capability.
   105→
   106→### Batch Operations
   107→
   108→Use `--batch` with `create-skill` and `test-skill` to process multiple skills at once. Progress is checkpointed — use `--continue` to resume if interrupted.
   109→
   110→### Stack Skills + Individual Skills
   111→
   112→Stack skills focus on integration patterns. Individual skills focus on API surface. Use both together for maximum coverage.
   113→
   114→### The Loop
   115→
   116→After each sprint's refactor, run `@Ferris US` to regenerate changed components. Export updates CLAUDE.md automatically. Skill generation becomes routine — like running tests.
   117→
   118→---
   119→
   120→## Troubleshooting
   121→
   122→### Common Issues
   123→
   124→**"Forge halted: ast-grep not found"**
   125→Install ast-grep to unlock Forge mode: <https://ast-grep.github.io>
   126→
   127→**"No brief found"**
   128→Run `@Ferris BS` first to create a skill brief, or use `@Ferris QS` for brief-less generation.
   129→
   130→**"Ecosystem check: official skill exists"**
   131→An official skill already exists for this package. Consider installing it with `npx skills add` instead of generating your own.
   132→
   133→**Quick mode skills have lower confidence**
   134→Quick mode reads source without AST analysis. Install ast-grep to upgrade to Forge mode for structural truth (T1 confidence).
   135→
   136→---
   137→
   138→## Getting More Help
   139→
   140→- Run `/bmad-help` — analyzes your current state and suggests what to do next
   141→  (e.g. `/bmad-help my batch creation failed halfway, how do I resume?`)
   142→  *Provided by the [BMad Method](https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD) — not available in standalone SKF installations.*
   143→- Run `@Ferris SF` to check your current tier and tool availability
   144→- Review `forge-config.yaml` for runtime configuration
   145→- Check module configuration in your BMAD settings
   146→

     5→
     6→# Agents Reference
     7→
     8→SKF includes 1 specialized agent:
     9→
    10→---
    11→
    12→## Ferris — Skill Architect & Integrity Guardian
    13→
    14→**ID:** `_bmad/skf/agents/forger.md`
    15→**Icon:** ⚒️
    16→
    17→**Role:**
    18→Sole agent managing the entire skill compilation lifecycle. Ferris extracts, compiles, validates, and packages agent skills from code repositories, documentation, and developer discourse.
    19→
    20→**When to Use:**
    21→Ferris handles all SKF workflows. You always interact with Ferris — he switches modes based on which workflow you invoke.
    22→
    23→**Key Capabilities:**
    24→- AST analysis via ast-grep for structural truth
    25→- QMD knowledge search for temporal context and evidence
    26→- agentskills.io specification compliance and validation
    27→- GitHub source navigation and package-to-repo resolution
    28→- Cross-knowledge synthesis for stack skills and integration patterns
    29→
    30→**Workflow-Driven Modes:**
    31→
    32→| Mode | Behavior | Workflows |
    33→|------|----------|-----------|
    34→| **Architect** | Exploratory, structural, assembling | BS, CS, QS, SS, AN |
    35→| **Surgeon** | Precise, semantic diffing, preserves [MANUAL] | US |
    36→| **Audit** | Judgmental, drift reports, completeness scoring | AS, TS |
    37→| **Delivery** | Packaging, platform-aware, ecosystem-ready | EX |
    38→
    39→**Communication Style:**
    40→- During work: structured reports with AST citations, no metaphor
    41→- At transitions: forge language, brief and warm
    42→- On completion: quiet craftsman's pride
    43→- On errors: direct and actionable
    44→
    45→**Menu:**
    46→
    47→```
    48→⚒️ Ferris — Skill Forge
    49→
    50→START HERE:
    51→  [SF] Setup Forge — Initialize your forge environment
    52→  [AN] Analyze Source — Discover what to skill
    53→
    54→CREATE:
    55→  [BS] Brief Skill — Design a skill scope
    56→  [CS] Create Skill — Compile a skill from brief
    57→  [QS] Quick Skill — Fast skill, no brief needed
    58→  [SS] Stack Skill — Consolidated project stack skill
    59→
    60→MAINTAIN:
    61→  [US] Update Skill — Regenerate after changes
    62→  [AS] Audit Skill — Check for drift
    63→  [TS] Test Skill — Verify completeness
    64→
    65→DELIVER:
    66→  [EX] Export Skill — Package for distribution
    67→
    68→[WS] Workflow Status
    69→[FM] Full Menu
    70→```
    71→
    72→**Memory:**
    73→Ferris has a sidecar (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/`) that persists user preferences and tool availability across sessions.
    74→

     1→---
     2→title: Skill Forge (SKF)
     3→description: AST-verified, provenance-backed agent skills from code repositories, documentation, and developer discourse
     4→template: splash
     5→hero:
     6→  title: Skill Forge (SKF)
     7→  tagline: AST-verified, provenance-backed agent skills from code repositories, documentation, and developer discourse
     8→  actions:
     9→    - text: Getting Started
    10→      link: ./getting-started/
    11→      icon: right-arrow
    12→      variant: primary
    13→    - text: View on GitHub
    14→      link: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge
    15→      icon: external
    16→---
    17→
    18→## What is Skill Forge?
    19→
    20→Skill Forge is an automated skill compiler for the AI agent ecosystem. It transforms code repositories, documentation, and developer discourse into [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io)-compliant, version-pinned, provenance-backed agent skills. Every instruction traces to verifiable sources — zero hallucination tolerance.
    21→
    22→- **AST-Verified**: Structural truth via ast-grep — no guessing, no hallucination.
    23→- **Provenance-Backed**: Every claim traces to source code with file and line references.
    24→- **Progressive Tiers**: Quick (no setup) → Forge (ast-grep) → Deep (QMD knowledge).
    25→- **Ecosystem-First**: Checks for official skills before generating community ones.
    26→
    27→## Quick Install
    28→
    29→Requires [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) >= 22.
    30→
    31→**Standalone:**
    32→
    33→```bash
    34→npx bmad-module-skill-forge install
    35→```
    36→
    37→**Or as a custom module with BMad Method** (for the full development workflow):
    38→
    39→```bash
    40→npx bmad-method install    # select "Add custom modules" and point to src/
    41→```
    42→
    43→Then interact with the forge agent:
    44→
    45→```
    46→@Ferris SF    # Setup your forge environment
    47→@Ferris QS    # Quick Skill — generate in under a minute
    48→```
    49→
    50→See the [Getting Started](./getting-started.md) guide for all installation methods.
    51→
    52→## Core Workflows
    53→
    54→| Workflow | Trigger | Purpose |
    55→|----------|---------|---------|
    56→| [Setup Forge](/workflows/#setup-forge-sf) | SF | Initialize forge, detect tools, set tier |
    57→| [Brief Skill](/workflows/#brief-skill-bs) | BS | Scope and design a skill |
    58→| [Create Skill](/workflows/#create-skill-cs) | CS | Compile a skill from a brief |
    59→| [Quick Skill](/workflows/#quick-skill-qs) | QS | Fast skill, no brief needed |
    60→| [Stack Skill](/workflows/#stack-skill-ss) | SS | Consolidated project stack skill |
    61→| [Update Skill](/workflows/#update-skill-us) | US | Regenerate after source changes |
    62→| [Audit Skill](/workflows/#audit-skill-as) | AS | Drift detection |
    63→| [Test Skill](/workflows/#test-skill-ts) | TS | Verify completeness |
    64→| [Export Skill](/workflows/#export-skill-ex) | EX | Package for distribution |
    65→| [Analyze Source](/workflows/#analyze-source-an) | AN | Discover what to skill |
    66→
    67→## Documentation
    68→
    69→- **[Getting Started](./getting-started.md)** — Installation, prerequisites, first steps
    70→- **[Architecture](./architecture.md)** — Output format, confidence model, tiers, tool ecosystem
    71→- **[Agents](./agents.md)** — Ferris agent: modes, capabilities, communication style
    72→- **[Workflows](./workflows.md)** — All 10 workflows with commands and connection diagram
    73→- **[Examples](./examples.md)** — Real-world scenarios, tips, and troubleshooting
    74→
    75→## Support
    76→
    77→- **Issues**: [GitHub Issues](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues)
    78→
```

## Pre-Release Audit Report: README.md & docs/

### CRITICAL — Would cause user confusion or failure

| # | Issue | Files | Tiers Affected |
|---|-------|-------|----------------|
| **C1** | **Ghost `forge-config.yaml`** — eliminated during setup-forge consolidation, but still referenced as if it exists | `docs/getting-started.md:198`, `docs/examples.md:144`, `docs/workflows.md:22` | All |
| **C2** | **README Module Structure: phantom `src/docs/`** — tree shows `docs/` inside `src/`, but no `src/docs/` exists. Docs live at repo root `docs/` | `README.md:188-193` | All |
| **C3** | **README Module Structure: phantom `skillforge/` nesting** — tree shows `workflows/skillforge/{name}/` but source is `src/workflows/{name}/` (no `skillforge/` subfolder). That nesting only exists in installed path | `README.md:202-213` | All |
| **C4** | **Source `preferences.yaml` template missing `tier_override`** — README:177 and architecture.md:53 tell users to set it, but the shipped template at `src/forger/preferences.yaml` doesn't have the field. Only users who ran `setup-forge` would get it (step-02-write-config creates it on first run) | `src/forger/preferences.yaml`, `README.md:177`, `docs/architecture.md:53` | All |

### HIGH — Inconsistency that would mislead users

| # | Issue | Files | Tiers Affected |
|---|-------|-------|----------------|
| **H1** | **Mode assignments disagree** — README puts CS, QS, SS in **Surgeon**; docs/agents.md and docs/workflows.md put them in **Architect**. README is the outlier | `README.md:85-87` vs `docs/agents.md:33-37` + `docs/workflows.md:52` | All |
| **H2** | **Knowledge fragment count: "8" vs "9"** — README:55 says "8 reusable fragments", README:201 says "9 fragments". Actual count is 9 .md files | `README.md:55` vs `README.md:201` | All |
| **H3** | **Tool count header wrong** — architecture.md says "### 4 MCP Tools" but the table lists 5 (gh_bridge, skill-check, tessl, ast_bridge, qmd_bridge) | `docs/architecture.md:236` | All |
| **H4** | **forge-tier.yaml tier comment misleading** — says `Quick = no tools` but Quick actually requires `gh` + `skill-check` + `tessl` per all documentation | `src/forger/forge-tier.yaml:12`, `src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-02-write-config.md:68` | Quick |

### MEDIUM — Minor inconsistencies

| # | Issue | Files | Tiers Affected |
|---|-------|-------|----------------|
| **M1** | **`doc_fetcher` is vaporware** — referenced in architecture.md as optional addon, mentioned for T3 confidence. Zero implementation exists in source (`grep doc_fetcher src/` = 0 matches) | `docs/architecture.md:84,246` | Deep |
| **M2** | **SF missing from docs/agents.md mode table** — README and docs/workflows.md assign SF to Architect mode, but agents.md omits SF entirely | `docs/agents.md:33-37` | All |
| **M3** | **README implies `src/README.md` exists** — the Module Structure tree includes it but no such file exists | `README.md:186` | N/A |

---

**Summary: 4 critical, 4 high, 3 medium issues.** The `forge-config.yaml` ghost and the README module structure tree are the biggest risks — users will look for files/directories that don't exist.

# Cross-Workflow Dependency Audit Report

## Summary

6 broken or inconsistent contracts were found. They are ordered by severity of impact.

## Issue 1 — CRITICAL: forge-tier.yaml field name `tier` vs `detected_tier` mismatch

**Producer:** setup-forge/steps-c/step-02-write-config.md (line 69)
**Consumers:** test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md (line 115), audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md (line 84), update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md (line 88)

setup-forge writes forge-tier.yaml with the field named `tier`:

```yaml
tier: {calculated_tier}
```

The test-skill consumer reads `detected_tier` by name:

```
Read `detected_tier` value (Quick, Forge, or Deep)
```

The audit-skill consumer reads `tier level: Quick / Forge / Deep` without naming the field explicitly, but the update-skill consumer explicitly reads `forge_tier` from the file:

```
Extract: `forge_tier` (Quick, Forge, or Deep), available tools
```

So the single written field name `tier` is being read by three different consumers using three different names: `detected_tier` (test-skill), `tier` (audit-skill), and `forge_tier` (update-skill).

The create-skill consumer (step-01-load-brief.md line 64) reads `tier` correctly, matching what setup-forge writes. But test-skill and update-skill are misaligned. The variable `detected_tier` in test-skill and `forge_tier` in update-skill do not match the actual YAML key `tier` in the file.

## Issue 2 — CRITICAL: metadata.json missing `source_root` field; consumers expect it

**Producer:** create-skill/data/skill-sections.md (lines 96-124) — the canonical metadata.json schema
**Consumers:** update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md (line 78), audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md (line 99), test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md (line 134)

The canonical metadata.json schema written by create-skill contains these fields: `name`, `version`, `skill_type`, `source_authority`, `source_repo`, `source_commit`, `confidence_tier`, `spec_version`, `generation_date`, `exports`, `tool_versions`, `stats`, `dependencies`, `compatibility`.

There is no `source_root` or `source_path` field in this schema.

However:

- update-skill step-01 (line 78) explicitly reads `source_root` from metadata.json: `Extract: name, skill_type, version, generation_date, confidence_tier, source_root`
- audit-skill step-01 (line 99) reads `source_root` from metadata.json: `source_root — Original source code path`
- test-skill step-01 (line 134) reads `source_path` from metadata.json: `source_path — path to source code (if present)`

Both `source_root` and `source_path` are absent from the schema in `skill-sections.md`. The consumers will find this field missing on every skill produced by create-skill. Both audit-skill and update-skill degrade to asking the user for the source path when the provenance map is also missing, but they would encounter this on every normal run since the field is never written.

The test-skill consumer notes `(if present)` — but audit-skill and update-skill treat `source_root` from metadata.json as their primary path resolution mechanism before falling back to the provenance map.

The provenance map (`provenance-map.json`) does contain `source_repo` and `source_commit` (not `source_root`), so even that fallback path has a field name mismatch: audit-skill step-01 (line 119) reads `source_root` from the provenance map, but provenance-map.json has `source_repo` per the schema in `skill-sections.md` (line 155).

## Issue 3 — CRITICAL: export-skill reads a file that setup-forge explicitly eliminated

**Producer:** setup-forge (does not write forge-config.yaml — explicitly eliminated)
**Consumer:** export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md (line 114)

The setup-forge workflow explicitly eliminated `forge-config.yaml` and consolidated everything into `forge-tier.yaml` (setup-forge/workflow-plan-setup-forge.md line 177: "Eliminated: `forge-config.yaml` — consolidated into forge-tier.yaml to prevent drift"; validation-report.md line 373 confirms this).

Yet export-skill step-01 (line 114) reads from it:

```
Load `{forge_data_folder}/forge-config.yaml` (if exists):
- Check `passive_context` setting
```

export-skill steps 03 and 04 both reference `passive_context` from `forge-config.yaml` as the gate that controls whether snippet generation and CLAUDE.md injection are performed. Since `forge-config.yaml` will never be written, the `passive_context` check will always fail to find the file. The steps treat a missing file as "passive_context: true" (default enabled) so the feature still works, but the configuration path is permanently broken — users can never set `passive_context: false` because the file that would hold that setting is never created.

Additionally, the path is wrong: step-01 reads from `{forge_data_folder}/forge-config.yaml` but the now-eliminated file was at the sidecar path (`_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/`), not `forge-data/`.

## Issue 4 — HIGH: skill-brief.yaml field name mismatch: `scope` object vs flat fields

**Producer:** brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md (lines 83-89)
**Consumer:** create-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-brief.md (lines 88-100)

The brief-skill producer writes the scope as a nested object:

```yaml
scope:
  type: "{approved scope type}"
  include:
    - "{approved include patterns}"
  exclude:
    - "{approved exclude patterns}"
  notes: "{approved scope notes or empty string}"
```

The create-skill consumer validates for the required field named `scope` (line 93) and calls it "what to extract (e.g., 'all public exports', specific modules)". The description treats `scope` as a single string, not an object.

However — the schema file (`brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md`) correctly defines scope as an object, and so does the `analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`. The discrepancy is in create-skill step-01's validation description at line 93 which describes `scope` as a string-typed field with example values like `"all public exports"`. The downstream extraction step (step-03) and ecosystem-check (step-02) will read whatever is in context, so the nested object would be passed through. This is a documentation inconsistency rather than a runtime failure, but it would cause the brief validation check in step-01 to potentially flag valid `scope` objects as non-compliant if the validator interprets the description literally.

## Issue 5 — HIGH: QMD collection registry entries written with inconsistent schemas between producers

**Producer 1:** brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md (lines 121-129) — type `"brief"`
**Producer 2:** create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md (lines 163+) — type `"temporal"`, adds `extraction_fingerprint` field
**Producer 3:** create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md (lines 151-158) — type `"extraction"`
**Consumer:** setup-forge/steps-c/step-03-auto-index.md cross-references registry vs live QMD; audit-skill/steps-c/step-02-re-index.md (lines 120-134) reads registry for Deep tier enrichment

The three registry entry schemas produced are not consistent:

brief-skill writes:
```yaml
- name: "{skill-name}-brief"
  type: "brief"
  source_workflow: "brief-skill"
  skill_name: "{skill-name}"
  created_at: "{current ISO date}"
```

create-skill (step-07) writes:
```yaml
- name: "{name}-extraction"
  type: "extraction"
  source_workflow: "create-skill"
  skill_name: "{name}"
  created_at: "{current ISO date}"
```

create-skill (step-03b) writes entries with type `"temporal"` AND an additional `extraction_fingerprint` field not present in the other two schemas.

setup-forge step-03 performs hygiene by matching registry entries against live QMD collections using only `name` for cross-referencing, which works. But audit-skill step-02 (line 122) searches the registry with the condition: `skill_name` matches AND `type` is `"extraction"`. This means the `brief` and `temporal` typed entries are ignored by audit-skill, which is the intended behavior. The inconsistency of the extra `extraction_fingerprint` field in temporal entries is not consumed anywhere outside step-03b itself — it is written but never read by any consuming step. This is a dead field.

## Issue 6 — MEDIUM: test-skill produces a test report; export-skill has no step that reads it

**Producer:** test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md — writes `{output_folder}/test-report-{skill_name}.md` with frontmatter including `testResult: pass|fail` and `nextWorkflow`
**Consumer:** export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md

The workflow documentation describes test-skill as a quality gate for export-skill. However, export-skill step-01 contains no instruction to check for or read a test report. It loads `SKILL.md` and `metadata.json`, validates their structure, and proceeds. There is no path check for `testResult` in the test report, no `score` threshold enforcement, and no halt condition if the test result is `fail`.

The test report's `nextWorkflow` field, which would be `export-skill` on a PASS, is written but never read by export-skill. The quality gate described in the documentation is entirely advisory — export-skill will package any skill regardless of whether test-skill was run or whether it passed or failed.

## Non-Issues Confirmed

The following potential issues were investigated and found to be sound:

- **brief-skill write path vs create-skill read path**: Both resolve to the same location. `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` equals `{project-root}/forge-data/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` because module.yaml defines `forge_data_folder` as `{project-root}/{value}` with default `forge-data`.
- **analyze-source → create-skill brief schema alignment**: The schema in `analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md` exactly matches the schema in `brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md` for all 9 required fields. Both write to `{forge_data_folder}/{unit-name}/skill-brief.yaml`.
- **quick-skill write outputs vs test-skill/export-skill inputs**: quick-skill writes `SKILL.md`, `context-snippet.md`, and `metadata.json` to `{skills_output_folder}/{repo_name}/`. Both test-skill and export-skill require only `SKILL.md` and `metadata.json` as hard-required files. The quick-skill `metadata.json` is written with the same key fields expected by test-skill (`skill_type`, `confidence_tier`) and export-skill (`name`, `version`, `skill_type`, `source_authority`, `exports`, `generation_date`, `confidence_tier`), though quick-skill generates them with less provenance.
- **setup-forge qmd_collections field**: setup-forge writes `qmd_collections: []` initially and all three consumers (brief-skill step-05, create-skill step-07, create-skill step-03b) correctly append to this array. The setup-forge step-03 hygiene reads it back accurately.
- **forge-tier.yaml `tools` subfields**: setup-forge writes `tools.ast_grep`, `tools.gh_cli`, `tools.qmd` as booleans. create-skill step-01 reads `tools` as a group (which tools are available) without naming specific subkeys, so this is compatible.

## Tier-Conditional Logic Audit: Confirmed Issues

### Issue A — forge-tier.yaml template comment contradicts tier-rules.md

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/forger/forge-tier.yaml`, line 11

**The comment reads:**
```
# Quick = gh + skill-check + tessl | Forge = + ast-grep | Deep = + QMD
```

**What tier-rules.md says:** Quick = no tools required (the default when no external tools are available). gh alone does not change the tier. The tiers are: Quick (baseline, no tools), Forge (ast-grep available), Deep (ast-grep + gh + qmd all available).

**Impact:** The comment is embedded in the live sidecar template written by setup-forge/step-02. Any developer reading this file to understand tier semantics gets wrong information. Additionally, `src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-02-write-config.md` (line 68) reproduces this same incorrect comment verbatim in the template it writes, so the mismatch propagates into every deployed instance.

**Affected files:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/forger/forge-tier.yaml` (line 11)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-02-write-config.md` (line 68, inside the yaml template block)

---

### Issue B — test-skill/step-01-init.md reads a field name that does not exist in forge-tier.yaml

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md`, Section 4

The step instructs the agent to read `detected_tier` from `forge-tier.yaml`. The forge-tier.yaml schema (written by setup-forge/step-02) stores this value under the key `tier:`, not `detected_tier:`.

**Consequence:** At runtime the agent reads a null/missing key and has no tier value to propagate forward. All downstream tier-conditional branches in test-skill (step-03-coverage-check tier-dependent extraction depth, step-04-coherence-check section 5b guard, step-05-score weight selection) operate on an undefined tier.

**Authoritative schema source:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/forger/forge-tier.yaml`, line 12 (`tier: ~`) and setup-forge/step-02-write-config.md line 69 (`tier: {calculated_tier}`).

**Affected file:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md`

---

### Issue C — test-skill/step-05-score.md contains a duplicate "External Validation unavailable" paragraph with contradictory redistribution weights

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md`, Section 3

The section contains two separate paragraphs describing the weight redistribution when external validation tools are unavailable. They give different numbers:

- First occurrence: Export Coverage 45%, Signature Accuracy 25%, Type Coverage 20%, External Validation 10% → redistributed to 45% / 25% / 20% (total: 90%, missing 10%)
- Second occurrence: same scenario → redistributed to 50% / 28% / 22% (total: 100%)

The first occurrence is arithmetically incorrect (weights do not sum to 100%). The second occurrence is internally consistent. Both paragraphs cannot simultaneously be correct instructions.

**Impact:** When skill-check or tessl is unavailable, the agent must pick one redistribution formula. The presence of two contradictory formulas creates non-deterministic scoring behavior across runs.

**Affected file:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md`

---

### Issue D — progressive-capability.md scoring weights disagree with test-skill/step-05-score.md

**Files:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/progressive-capability.md`, Example 4
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md`

`progressive-capability.md` Example 4 documents Quick-tier naive mode scoring as: Export Coverage 50%, Signature Accuracy 30%, Type Coverage 20%. There is no External Validation category in this breakdown.

`step-05-score.md` implements: Export Coverage 45%, Signature Accuracy 25%, Type Coverage 20%, External Validation 10%.

The knowledge document predates the external validators integration (skill-check and tessl were added in commit `9bda903`). The knowledge document was not updated to reflect the new four-category weight structure. Any developer consulting `progressive-capability.md` to understand scoring behavior will get stale numbers.

**Affected file (needs update):** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/progressive-capability.md`

---

### Issue E — create-skill/step-04-enrich.md QMD collection type filter includes extraction collections

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-04-enrich.md`, Section 2

The step's pre-check for identifying which QMD collections contain temporal context filters for: `type != "brief"`. This passes both `"temporal"` collections (correct, these contain GitHub changelog/release data) and `"extraction"` collections (incorrect, these contain AST extraction results written by create-skill/step-07 and audit-skill/step-02).

The authoritative producer of temporal context collections is `brief-skill/step-05-write-brief.md` and `create-skill/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`, both of which register collections with `type: "temporal"`. Extraction collections registered by `create-skill/step-07-generate-artifacts.md` have `type: "extraction"`.

**Consequence:** At Deep tier, step-04 may pull AST extraction data from a prior run's extraction collection and attempt to use it as temporal (T2) enrichment context. This would produce T2-annotated entries that are not actually temporally sourced — corrupting the confidence tier of the enriched output.

The correct filter is `type == "temporal"` (exact match), not `type != "brief"`.

**Affected file:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-04-enrich.md`

---

### Issue F — quick-skill workflow never loads forge-tier.yaml and never applies tier_override

**Files:** All six steps under `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/`

Every other workflow that has an init step (create-skill, test-skill, audit-skill, update-skill, brief-skill, analyze-source, create-stack-skill) explicitly reads `forge-tier.yaml` and applies `preferences.yaml`'s `tier_override` setting.

None of the six quick-skill steps load forge-tier.yaml or read preferences.yaml. The workflow is hard-wired to Quick-tier behavior with no escape hatch.

**The specific gap:** A user who installs ast-grep and qmd, runs setup-forge (which writes `tier: Deep` to forge-tier.yaml), and then invokes the quick-skill workflow will receive Quick-tier output even if their `preferences.yaml` sets `tier_override: Forge` or `tier_override: Deep`. The tier_override mechanism is silently ignored.

This is distinct from the design choice to make quick-skill Quick-only. The issue is that the user's explicit override setting is not even consulted. If the design intent is that quick-skill always runs at Quick regardless of tier_override, that constraint should be documented and enforced with a visible notice. If the intent is that quick-skill should respect tier_override, the step-01 init needs a forge-tier.yaml load.

**Affected directory:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/`

### Issue G — analyze-source/step-02 and step-04 reference AST-tier depth without degradation fallback

**Files:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-02-scan-project.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-04-map-and-detect.md`

Both steps describe tier-dependent analysis depth (Quick: file structure / Forge: + manifest parsing / Deep: + config analysis in step-02; Quick: file-level exports / Forge: parse export statements / Deep: + semantic in step-04). Neither step includes an explicit "AST tool unavailable" degradation warning.

Compare with the equivalent pattern in:
- `create-skill/step-03-extract.md`: includes an explicit "If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier" section that instructs the agent to emit a warning and degrade to Quick-tier behavior
- `create-stack-skill/step-04-parallel-extract.md`: same explicit pattern with "degrade to Quick extraction for that component"

analyze-source steps have no such guard. If the agent enters a Forge or Deep tier run of analyze-source but ast-grep fails or is unavailable, there is no instruction to fall back — the step silently omits the AST-tier depth without any notice to the user or downstream steps.

**Impact:** Downstream consumers of analyze-source output (brief-skill uses the analyze-source report as input) may receive incomplete structural data without knowing it was generated at degraded depth.

**Affected files:**
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-02-scan-project.md`
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-04-map-and-detect.md`

## WORKFLOW: create-skill (9 steps including step-03b)

### ISSUE CS-1: `step-03b` is undocumented in the workflow plan and data flow table

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/workflow-plan-create-skill.md` lines 305–328 (the Data Flow table) vs. `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` line 4 (frontmatter `nextStepFile: './step-03b-fetch-temporal.md'`)

The plan's Step Sequence Table (lines 239–247) lists only 8 numbered steps with no step-03b. However step-03's actual `nextStepFile` points to `step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`, and step-03b's `nextStepFile` points to `step-04-enrich.md`. The plan is an orphaned snapshot — the extra step was inserted without updating the plan document.

This is a documentation-only mismatch (the actual step files chain correctly), but the plan's Step Action Plans and Data Flow sections both skip step-03b entirely, making the plan inaccurate for anyone relying on it.

### ISSUE CS-2: `metadata.json` does not contain `source_root` — consumed by downstream workflows as if it does

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` lines 96–124 (the metadata.json schema definition) vs. `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` line 78 and `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` line 99.

**Broken link:** The canonical `metadata.json` schema in `skill-sections.md` defines these fields: `name`, `version`, `skill_type`, `source_authority`, `source_repo`, `source_commit`, `confidence_tier`, `spec_version`, `generation_date`, `exports`, `tool_versions`, `stats`, `dependencies`, `compatibility`. The field `source_root` (the resolved local or remote path to source code at extraction time) is **absent from the schema**.

- `update-skill/step-01-init.md` line 78 states it extracts `source_root` from `metadata.json`: "Extract: `name`, `skill_type` (single or stack), `version`, `generation_date`, `confidence_tier`, `source_root`"
- `audit-skill/step-01-init.md` line 99 also expects to extract `source_root` from `metadata.json`: "`source_root` — Original source code path"

`update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md` line 64 uses this field as the source path for ephemeral clone decisions. `audit-skill/step-01-init.md` lines 119–120 use it to resolve the source code path. Both workflows will silently receive `undefined`/null for `source_root` and fall back to asking the user, but the data contract states extraction of a field that `create-skill` never writes.

Note: `source_repo` (the GitHub URL/owner-repo format from the brief) IS in the schema, but `source_root` (the resolved local path post-extraction) is not. This distinction matters because `update-skill/step-03` uses `source_root` explicitly to decide between local-path and remote-URL processing paths.

### ISSUE CS-3: `step-05-compile.md` line 211 states 7 artifacts must be assembled but `extraction-rules.yaml` has no assembly instructions

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md` line 211 (Critical Step Completion Note) and the `skill-sections.md` data file.

The `skill-sections.md` data file defines schemas for `provenance-map.json`, `evidence-report.md`, `SKILL.md`, `context-snippet.md`, `metadata.json`, and `references/`, but contains **no schema or format definition for `extraction-rules.yaml`**. Step-05 has sections for SKILL.md (§2), context-snippet.md (§3), metadata.json (§4), references/ (§5), provenance-map.json (§6), evidence-report.md (§7) — but no section for assembling `extraction-rules.yaml`. The step's completion note counts it as one of 7 required artifacts, yet no instructions exist for building it. **Broken link:** The workflow plan's Step Sequence table lists 6 steps: 01 through 06, with step 04 as `coherence-check` directly chaining to step 05 as `score`. The File Structure listing (lines 240–251) shows only 6 step files and does not include `step-04b-external-validators.md`. The Interaction Flow diagram (line 210) also shows `[04] coherence-check (auto) → [05] score (auto)` with no step-04b. Yet `step-04-coherence-check.md` frontmatter has `nextStepFile: './step-04b-external-validators.md'` and `step-04b-external-validators.md` has `nextStepFile: './step-05-score.md'`. The plan is a stale snapshot.

This causes a real data contract issue: the workflow plan's Data Flow section (line 220) states step-05 reads from "coverage + coherence findings" — it does not document that step-05 also reads `external_score` from step-04b. Any developer following the plan to understand step-05's inputs will have an incomplete picture.

### ISSUE TS-2: `step-04b` reads `skillDir` from `{outputFile}` frontmatter, but `skillDir` is never written to the frontmatter

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md` lines 56–58 vs. `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` lines 144–156 and `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/templates/test-report-template.md`.

**Broken link (actual runtime failure):** Step-04b section 1 states "Read `{outputFile}` frontmatter to get the skill directory path (`skillDir`)." The test-report-template defines these frontmatter fields: `workflowType`, `skillName`, `testMode`, `forgeTier`, `testResult`, `score`, `threshold`, `testDate`, `stepsCompleted`, `nextWorkflow`. Step-01-init creates the output document with exactly those same fields in its initial frontmatter block (lines 144–156). There is **no `skillDir` field** in the template or in the init-written frontmatter.

Step-04b will read a `null`/empty `skillDir` from the frontmatter and then pass that empty value directly to the tool commands:
```bash
npx skill-check check {skillDir} --format json --no-security-scan
npx -y tessl skill review {skillDir}
```

Both tool invocations will fail with an invalid or empty path argument. The step has no fallback for this case — it only handles tool unavailability (`skill_check_score: N/A`), not an empty/missing path. `skill-check` and `tessl` will either error out on the empty arg or scan the current directory instead of the skill directory. This is a definite runtime breakage for every test-skill run that reaches step-04b.

**The fix required:** Step-01-init must add a `skillPath` (or `skillDir`) field to the frontmatter it writes into the output document, populated from the resolved skill directory path stored as `{skill_path}`.

### ISSUE TS-3: `step-06-report.md` does not collect or mention `external_score` / External Validation gaps — the gap source list is incomplete

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md` lines 59–71 vs. `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md` section 5 (External Validation output).

**Missing consumption:** Step-06 section 1 ("Collect All Issues") lists sources as: "From Coverage Analysis (step 03)" and "From Coherence Analysis (step 04)". It never mentions the External Validation section appended by step-04b, and the severity rules in step-06 section 2 do not include external validation findings (e.g., tessl suggestions, skill-check diagnostics) as gap sources. The `{scoringRulesFile}` severity table maps `Critical/High/Medium/Low/Info` to coverage and coherence finding types only. External validator warnings (tessl `judge_suggestions`, skill-check unresolved `diagnostics`) are never elevated to the Gap Report, even though they represent actionable skill deficiencies.

This is a data flow omission — step-04b writes findings to the output document, but step-06 reads only from steps 03 and 04 when compiling gaps.

## WORKFLOW: quick-skill (6 steps)

### ISSUE QS-1: `metadata.json` produced by quick-skill is missing `skill_type` — required by `export-skill` and `test-skill`

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` lines 116–129 and `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` lines 62–73 vs. `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-skill.md` line 91.

**Broken link:** Quick-skill's `metadata.json` template defines these fields: `name`, `version`, `source_authority`, `source_repo`, `source_package`, `language`, `generated_by`, `generated_date`, `exports_count`, `confidence`. The field `skill_type` is absent from both the compiled metadata.json and the skill-template.md format spec.

`export-skill/step-01-load-skill.md` line 91 performs a hard validation check: "metadata.json contains required fields: `name`, `version`, `skill_type`, `source_authority`, `exports`, `generation_date`, `confidence_tier`". If a quick-skill-generated `metadata.json` is passed to export-skill, the validation fails on `skill_type` (absent) and `exports` (the field is named `exports_count` in quick-skill, not `exports`) and `confidence_tier` (named `confidence` in quick-skill). The export would hard-halt at step-01 with "Export cannot proceed. Missing or invalid: skill_type, exports, confidence_tier".

Additionally `test-skill/step-01-init.md` line 135 reads `skill_type` from metadata.json to determine `naive` vs `contextual` mode — a quick-skill-produced metadata would cause it to default to naive mode silently, which is correct behavior, but the field mismatch still indicates the metadata contracts are not aligned across workflows.

Three field mismatches between quick-skill output and downstream consumers:
- quick-skill writes `confidence`, export-skill expects `confidence_tier`
- quick-skill writes `exports_count` (a number), export-skill expects `exports` (an array)
- quick-skill does not write `skill_type` at all

### ISSUE QS-2: `context-snippet.md` format mismatch between quick-skill template and the standard format

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` lines 50–56 vs. `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` lines 83–89.

**Name mismatch:** Quick-skill template defines the context-snippet format as:
```
{skill_name}: {top-5 exports as comma-separated list}
  → {skills_output_folder}/{skill_name}/SKILL.md
```

The standard create-skill format (used by export-skill when regenerating snippets) is:
```
{skill-name} -> skills/{skill-name}/
  exports: {comma-separated top 10 function names}
```

The arrow character differs (`→` vs `->`), the first line format differs (quick uses `name: exports` while create uses `name -> directory/`), and the second line format differs. If `export-skill` attempts to re-use or compare existing context-snippet.md files produced by quick-skill, the format parse will fail. This is a format contract mismatch between two producers of the same artifact type.

### ISSUE US-1: `step-06-write.md` references `update_type: "incremental"` but step-01's degraded mode should set `"full"` — no conditional logic

**Location:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-06-write.md` lines 91–99.

**Missing field logic:** The provenance map update operation metadata hardcodes `"update_type": "incremental"` regardless of whether the workflow was running in degraded mode (no provenance map, full re-extraction). This is a minor semantic issue rather than a hard breakage, but it creates false provenance data.

## 2. NAMING CONVENTION VIOLATIONS

The BMAD convention requires the frontmatter `name` field to match the filename stem (e.g., file `step-03-auto-index.md` → `name: 'step-03-auto-index'`).

**One mismatch found:**

| File | Filename Stem | Frontmatter `name` | Status |
|------|---------------|---------------------|--------|
| `/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-03-auto-index.md` | `step-03-auto-index` | `step-03-qmd-hygiene` | MISMATCH |

The file was renamed on disk but the frontmatter `name`, body title ("Step 3: QMD Collection Hygiene"), and description were updated to reflect the new purpose, leaving the filename out of sync. This file is correctly chained — `step-02-write-config.md` references `./step-03-auto-index.md` (the filename), so the chain works at runtime, but the `name` field is semantically incorrect.

All other 65 step files have `name` fields that match their filename stems exactly.

---

| `analyze-source/workflow.md` | YES |

Whether this is a required field depends on how BMAD processes workflow manifests. Given 6 of 10 workflows include it, its absence in the other 4 is likely an oversight.
