## 4. FRONTMATTER VARIABLE VIOLATIONS

The BMAD standard rule: only variables USED in the step body may appear in frontmatter; all file references in the body must use `{variable}` format corresponding to a declared frontmatter key.

### 4a. `{sidecar_path}` used in body but NOT declared in frontmatter

This is the most widespread pattern issue. `{sidecar_path}` is used in step bodies as a path variable but is never declared as a frontmatter key in any step file that uses it. The variable appears to be a context-injected runtime variable rather than a declared frontmatter file reference.

Files using `{sidecar_path}` in body without frontmatter declaration:

| File | Body Usage | Frontmatter Declaration |
|------|-----------|------------------------|
| `/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-brief.md` | `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` | **None** — has `forgeTierFile` and `preferencesFile` instead |
| `/src/workflows/analyze-source/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` | **None** |
| `/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`, `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` | **None** |
| `/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md` | `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml` | **None** |
| `/src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` | `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` | **None** — has `forgeTierFile` using `{project-root}` literal path |
| `/src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md` | `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml` | **None** |
| `/src/workflows/audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`, `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` | **None** |
| `/src/workflows/audit-skill/steps-c/step-02-re-index.md` | `{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml` | **None** |
| `/src/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md` | `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` | `forgeTierFile: '{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml'` — declares it here only |

**Exception — `test-skill/step-01-init.md`:** This file correctly declares `sidecarFile: '{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml'` in frontmatter, and `{sidecar_path}` is used in the body. However, the frontmatter key name is `sidecarFile`, not `sidecar_path` — so `{sidecar_path}` in the body is still an undeclared variable, even though `{sidecarFile}` is declared.

### 4b. `{project-root}` hardcoded path in frontmatter (forbidden pattern)

Per BMAD standards, hardcoded paths in frontmatter are forbidden — all file references must use `{variable}` format. However, `{project-root}` appears to be a special system-level variable rather than a literal path, so this is likely permitted. The issue is the inconsistency:

| File | Frontmatter | Issue |
|------|------------|-------|
| `create-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-brief.md` | `forgeTierFile: '{project-root}/_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/forge-tier.yaml'` | Uses `{project-root}` literal, inconsistent with `{sidecar_path}` used in body |
| `brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` | `forgeTierFile: '{project-root}/_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/forge-tier.yaml'` | Same issue |

Both files use `{project-root}` in frontmatter to declare the forge-tier file path, but then reference the same file via `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml` in the body, indicating two different variable systems are in play for the same path.

     3→description: 'Run external validation tools (skill-check, tessl) and capture scores for scoring step'

     8→---
     9→
    10→# Step 4b: External Validators
    11→
    12→## STEP GOAL:
    13→
    14→Run external validation tools (`skill-check` and `tessl`) against the skill directory, capture their scores and findings, and append results to the test report. These tools catch complementary issues that internal coverage and coherence checks miss: `skill-check` validates spec compliance while `tessl` evaluates content quality and actionability.
    15→
    16→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    17→
    18→### Universal Rules:
    19→
    20→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    21→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
    22→- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
    23→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    24→
    25→### Role Reinforcement:
    26→
    27→- ✅ You are a skill auditor running external quality gates
    28→- ✅ Both tools are optional — unavailability is a skip, never a halt
    29→- ✅ Report scores factually — do not editorialize on the results
    30→
    31→### Step-Specific Rules:
    32→
    33→- 🎯 Run each tool independently — one tool's failure does not affect the other
    34→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to halt the workflow if either tool is unavailable or fails
    35→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to modify skill content — this step only measures and reports
    36→- ⚠️ Ignore non-zero exit codes if the tool produces parseable output
    37→
    38→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    39→
    40→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    41→- 💾 Append External Validation section to {outputFile}
    42→- 📖 Update stepsCompleted in {outputFile}
    43→- 📖 Store scores in context for step-05 scoring
    44→
    45→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    46→
    47→- Available: skill directory path, testMode, forge tier from previous steps
    48→- Focus: External tool execution and score capture only
    49→- Limits: Do NOT modify skill content or recalculate internal scores
    50→- Dependencies: steps 03 and 04 must have completed
    51→
    52→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    53→
    54→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    55→
    56→### 1. Resolve Skill Directory
    57→
    58→Read {outputFile} frontmatter to get the skill directory path (`skillDir`).
    59→
    60→### 2. Run skill-check
    61→
    62→**Check availability:**
    63→
    64→```bash
    65→npx skill-check -h
    66→```
    67→
    68→If unavailable, record `skill_check_score: N/A` and skip to section 3.
    69→
    70→**Run validation:**
    71→
    72→```bash
    73→npx skill-check check {skillDir} --format json --no-security-scan
    74→```
    75→
    76→**Parse JSON output** to extract:
    77→- `qualityScore` — overall score (0-100)
    78→- `diagnostics[]` — any remaining issues
    79→- `errorCount` and `warningCount`
    80→
    81→**Note:** `skill-check` may return a non-zero exit code even when `errorCount` is 0. Always rely on the parsed JSON output, not the shell exit code.
    82→
    83→Store in context: `skill_check_score`, `skill_check_diagnostics`
    84→
    85→**If skill-check fails entirely:** Record `skill_check_score: N/A`, log warning, continue.
    86→
    87→### 3. Run tessl
    88→
    89→**Check availability:**
    90→
    91→```bash
    92→npx -y tessl --version
    93→```
    94→
    95→If unavailable, record `tessl_score: N/A` and skip to section 4.
    96→
    97→**Run review:**
    98→
    99→```bash
   100→npx -y tessl skill review {skillDir}
   101→```
   102→
   103→**Parse the output** to extract:
   104→- `description_score` — percentage (e.g., 100%)
   105→- `content_score` — percentage (e.g., 45%)
   106→- `average_score` — percentage (e.g., 73%)
   107→- `validation_result` — PASSED/FAILED
   108→- `judge_suggestions[]` — list of improvement suggestions
   109→
   110→The tessl output is human-readable text, not JSON. Parse the percentage values from lines like "Description: 100%", "Content: 45%", "Average Score: 73%".
   111→
   112→Store in context: `tessl_description_score`, `tessl_content_score`, `tessl_average_score`, `tessl_suggestions`
   113→
   114→**If tessl content score < 70%:** Flag a warning:
   115→
   116→"**Content quality warning:** tessl scored content at {score}%. This often indicates SKILL.md lacks inline actionable content (e.g., after split-body). Consider inlining Quick Start and common workflows directly in SKILL.md."
   117→
   118→**If tessl fails entirely:** Record `tessl_score: N/A`, log warning, continue.
   119→
   120→### 4. Calculate Combined External Score
   121→
   122→**If both tools ran:**
   123→
   124→```
   125→external_score = (skill_check_score + tessl_average_score) / 2
   126→```
   127→
   128→Note: `skill_check_score` is 0-100, `tessl_average_score` is 0-100%. Both are on the same scale.
   129→
   130→**If only one tool ran:** Use that tool's score as the external score.
   131→
   132→**If neither tool ran:** Record `external_score: N/A`. The scoring step will redistribute the external validation weight.
   133→
   134→### 5. Append External Validation to Output
   135→
   136→Append to `{outputFile}`:
   137→
   138→```markdown
   139→## External Validation
   140→
   141→### skill-check
   142→- **Available:** {yes/no}
   143→- **Quality Score:** {score}/100
   144→- **Errors:** {count}
   145→- **Warnings:** {count}
   146→- **Diagnostics:** {list or "none"}
   147→
   148→### tessl
   149→- **Available:** {yes/no}
   150→- **Validation:** {PASSED/FAILED}
   151→- **Description Score:** {score}%
   152→- **Content Score:** {score}%
   153→- **Average Score:** {score}%
   154→- **Suggestions:**
   155→{bulleted list of judge suggestions}
   156→
   157→### Combined External Score
   158→- **External Validation Score:** {external_score}%
   159→- **Tools used:** {list of tools that ran}
   160→```
   161→
   162→### 6. Report Results
   163→
   164→"**External validation complete.**
   165→
   166→| Tool | Score | Status |
   167→|------|-------|--------|
   168→| skill-check | {score}/100 | {available/skipped} |
   169→| tessl | {average}% | {available/skipped} |
   170→| **Combined** | **{external_score}%** | |
   171→
   172→{content quality warning if tessl content < 70%}
   173→
   174→**Proceeding to scoring...**"
   175→
   176→### 7. Auto-Proceed
   177→
   178→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   179→
   180→- After external validation is complete, update {outputFile} frontmatter stepsCompleted, then immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   181→
   182→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   183→
   184→- This is an auto-proceed validation step with no user choices
   185→- Tool unavailability is a skip, not a halt
   186→- Proceed directly to next step after external validation
   187→
   188→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   189→
   190→ONLY WHEN external validation results are appended to {outputFile} and scores are stored in context will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for scoring.
   191→
   192→---
   193→
   194→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   195→
   196→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   197→
   198→- skill-check executed and quality score captured (or skipped with N/A if unavailable)
   199→- tessl executed and description/content/average scores captured (or skipped with N/A)
   200→- Content quality warning raised if tessl content < 70%
   201→- Combined external score calculated
   202→- External Validation section appended to output
   203→- Scores stored in context for step-05
   204→- Auto-proceeded to step-05
   205→
   206→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   207→
   208→- Halting the workflow because a tool is unavailable or fails
   209→- Not recording N/A when a tool is skipped (step-05 needs to know)
   210→- Modifying skill content based on validation results
   211→- Not parsing tessl content score (the key insight this tool provides)
   212→- Fabricating scores not from actual tool output
   213→
   214→**Master Rule:** External validators inform — they do not block. Run what's available, capture the scores, report factually, and proceed.

    53→
    54→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    55→
    56→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
    57→
    58→### 1. Request Skill Path
    59→
    60→"**Which skill would you like to update?**

    69→
    70→### 2. Validate Required Artifacts
    71→
    72→**Check SKILL.md exists:**
    73→- Load `{resolved_skill_path}/SKILL.md`
    74→- If missing: **ABORT** — "No SKILL.md found at `{resolved_skill_path}`. Run create-skill first."
    75→

    79→- If missing: **ABORT** — "No metadata.json found. This skill may have been created manually. Run create-skill to generate provenance data."

    92→

    95→- **Forge:** AST structural extraction → T1 confidence
    96→- **Deep:** AST + QMD semantic enrichment → T1 + T2 confidence
    97→
    98→### 4. Load Provenance Map
    99→
   100→**Load `{forge_data_folder}/{skill_name}/provenance-map.json`:**

   117→
   118→### 5. Load [MANUAL] Section Inventory
   119→
   120→Load {manualSectionRulesFile} to understand [MANUAL] detection patterns.
   121→

   126→

     7→# Step 4: Enrich
     8→
     9→## STEP GOAL:
    10→
    11→To enrich the extraction inventory with temporal context from QMD knowledge searches — issues, PRs, changelogs, and migration notes that add T2-confidence annotations to extracted functions. Deep tier only; Quick and Forge tiers skip this step entirely.
    12→
    13→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    14→
    15→### Universal Rules:
    16→
    17→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    18→- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file

    20→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    21→
    22→### Role Reinforcement:
    23→
    24→- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing knowledge enrichment
    25→- ✅ QMD enrichment adds temporal depth — it never replaces AST extraction
    26→- ✅ Enrichment failure is graceful degradation, not an error
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Focus ONLY on QMD searches to annotate extracted functions
    31→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to modify extraction results — enrichment is additive only
    32→- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to begin compilation — that's step-05
    33→- 💬 QMD failures degrade gracefully — continue without enrichment
    34→- ⏱️ Quick and Forge tiers: skip this step entirely, auto-proceed
    35→
    36→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    37→
    38→- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
    39→- 💾 Enrichment annotations are added to extraction inventory in context
    40→- 📖 QMD results become T2-confidence annotations
    41→- 🚫 Never replace T1 extraction data with QMD findings

    47→- Limits: Do NOT modify extraction results, only annotate them

    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
    53→
    54→### 1. Check Tier Eligibility
    55→
    56→**If tier is Quick or Forge:**
    57→
    58→Auto-proceed silently. Display no message. Immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
    59→
    60→**If tier is Deep:**
    61→
    62→Continue to step 2.
    63→
    64→### 2. Collection Inventory Pre-check (Deep Tier Only)
    65→
    66→Before searching, check which QMD collections are available:
    67→
    68→1. Read the sidecar `forge-tier.yaml` to get registered `qmd_collections` entries
    69→2. Identify which collections contain temporal context — collections with `type` other than `"brief"` (e.g., issues, PRs, changelogs, architecture notes)
    70→3. **If no temporal collections exist** (only `"brief"` type or no collections at all): report this and auto-proceed. Display:
    71→
    72→"**Enrichment: no temporal collections available.**
    73→Only brief-type QMD collections found — no issues, PRs, or changelogs indexed. Enrichment skipped (expected for first-run skill creation). T2 enrichment becomes available when temporal context is indexed into QMD collections.
    74→
    75→Proceeding to compilation..."
    76→
    77→Then immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
    78→
    79→4. **If temporal collections exist:** Continue to step 3.
    80→
    81→### 3. QMD Enrichment Searches (Deep Tier Only)
    82→
    83→For each major exported function — limited to the **top-level public API surface** (typically 10-20 functions that will appear in the context-snippet), not all extracted exports — search the temporal QMD collections for context:
    84→
    85→**Search query construction:**
    86→
    87→For each function, derive the **module context** from the extraction inventory's source file path (e.g., `src/graph/neo4j/index.ts` → module context `graph neo4j`). This context improves search relevance by scoping results to the function's subsystem without adding extra queries.
    88→
    89→**Search targets per function:**
    90→
    91→1. **Issues/PRs:** `qmd_bridge.search("{module_context} {function_name}")` — find related discussions scoped by module context
    92→2. **Changelog entries:** `qmd_bridge.search("{function_name} changelog")` — find version history, breaking changes (kept generic — changelogs rarely use module paths)
    93→3. **Migration notes:** `qmd_bridge.vector_search("{module_context} {function_name} migration deprecated")` — find deprecation or migration context scoped by module (semantic search benefits from richer context)
    94→
    95→**For each QMD result:**
    96→
    97→- Create a T2 annotation: `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]`
    98→- Classify temporal relevance:
    99→  - **T2-past:** Historical context (why it was built, what it replaced)
   100→  - **T2-future:** Forward-looking context (planned changes, deprecation warnings)
   101→
   102→**Handling QMD failures:**
   103→
   104→- If qmd_bridge is unavailable: skip all enrichment, note in context
   105→- If individual search fails: skip that function's enrichment, continue with others
   106→- If QMD returns no results for a function: no annotation added (absence is normal)

   156→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   157→
   158→- Quick/Forge tiers: skipped silently with no output, auto-proceeded
   159→- Deep tier: collection inventory checked before searching
   160→- Deep tier: no temporal collections → reported and auto-proceeded (not silently skipped)
   161→- Deep tier: temporal collections exist → QMD searches performed per public API function (10-20, not all exports) with module context derived from extraction inventory source paths
   162→- T2 annotations added with proper provenance citations
   163→- Extraction data unmodified — enrichment is additive only
   164→- QMD failures handled gracefully (skip and continue)
   165→- Auto-proceeded to step-05
   166→
   167→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   168→
   169→- Halting on QMD unavailability or search failures
   170→- Modifying T1 extraction results with QMD data
   171→- Displaying skip messages for Quick/Forge tiers (should be silent)
   172→- Pre-emptively skipping all searches without checking the collection inventory
   173→- Searching all 800+ exports instead of scoping to public API surface (10-20)
   174→- Using bare function names without module context when source path is available in extraction inventory
   175→- Beginning compilation or SKILL.md assembly in this step
   176→- Not labeling QMD annotations as T2 confidence
   177→
   178→**Master Rule:** Enrichment is additive and optional. QMD adds temporal depth but never replaces structural extraction. Failures degrade gracefully.
   179→
```

    82→
    83→### 3. Apply Weight Distribution
    84→
    85→**Read testMode from {outputFile} frontmatter.**
    86→
    87→**IF naive mode — use redistributed weights:**
    88→
    89→| Category | Score | Weight | Weighted |
    90→|----------|-------|--------|----------|
    91→| Export Coverage | {N}% | 45% | {N * 0.45}% |
    92→| Signature Accuracy | {N}% | 25% | {N * 0.25}% |
    93→| Type Coverage | {N}% | 20% | {N * 0.20}% |
    94→| External Validation | {N}% | 10% | {N * 0.10}% |

    96→
    97→**IF contextual mode — use full weights:**
    98→
    99→| Category | Score | Weight | Weighted |
   100→|----------|-------|--------|----------|
   101→| Export Coverage | {N}% | 36% | {N * 0.36}% |
   102→| Signature Accuracy | {N}% | 22% | {N * 0.22}% |
   103→| Type Coverage | {N}% | 14% | {N * 0.14}% |
   104→| Coherence | {N}% | 18% | {N * 0.18}% |
   105→| External Validation | {N}% | 10% | {N * 0.10}% |
   106→| **Total** | | **100%** | **{sum}%** |
   107→
   108→**External Validation unavailable:**

   110→
   111→**External Validation unavailable:**
   112→If External Validation is N/A (neither skill-check nor tessl available), redistribute its 10% weight proportionally to the other active categories:
   113→- Naive: Export Coverage 50%, Signature Accuracy 28%, Type Coverage 22%
   114→- Contextual: Export Coverage 40%, Signature Accuracy 24%, Type Coverage 16%, Coherence 20%

   121→- Contextual + external N/A: Export Coverage 60%, Coherence 40%

   123→### 4. Determine Pass/Fail

   125→```

   127→score = sum of weighted category scores

     7→# Create Skill
     8→
     9→**Goal:** Compile a verified agent skill from a skill-brief.yaml and source code, producing an agentskills.io-compliant SKILL.md with provenance map, evidence report, and progressive disclosure references.
    10→
    11→**Your Role:** You are a skill compilation engine performing structural extraction and assembly. This workflow is mostly autonomous with two strategic confirmation gates — one after ecosystem check (if a match is found) and one after source extraction (to confirm findings before compilation). All other steps auto-proceed. Apply zero hallucination tolerance: every instruction in the output must trace to source code with a confidence tier citation.
    12→
    13→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    14→
    15→### Core Principles
    16→
    17→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step of the overall goal is a self contained instruction file that you will adhere too 1 file as directed at a time
    18→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only 1 current step file will be loaded, read, and executed to completion - never load future step files until told to do so
    19→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order, no skipping or optimization allowed
    20→- **Tier-Aware Execution**: Steps adapt behavior based on forge tier (Quick/Forge/Deep) loaded from sidecar at init
    21→- **Zero Hallucination**: Every claim requires a provenance citation — uncitable content is excluded, not guessed
    22→
    23→### Step Processing Rules
    24→
    25→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
    26→2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order, never deviate
    27→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: At confirmation gates (steps 02 and 03), halt and wait for user selection
    28→4. **AUTO-PROCEED**: All other steps proceed to the next automatically after completion
    29→5. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, load, read entire file, then execute the next step file
    30→
    31→### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
    32→
    33→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    34→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    35→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
    36→- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
    37→- 📋 **NEVER** create mental todo lists from future steps
    38→- ⚒️ **NEVER** include content in SKILL.md that cannot be cited to source code
    39→

     8→# Quick Skill
     9→
    10→**Goal:** The fastest path to a skill — accept a GitHub URL or package name, resolve to source, extract public API surface, and produce a best-effort SKILL.md with context snippet and metadata. No brief needed.
    11→
    12→> **Note:** Quick Skill is tier-unaware by design. It does not load forge-tier.yaml or check preferences.yaml for tier_override. All output is produced at community-tier quality regardless of available tools.
    13→
    14→**Your Role:** In addition to your name, communication_style, and persona, you are also a rapid skill compiler collaborating with a developer. This is a partnership, not a client-vendor relationship. You bring source analysis and skill document assembly expertise, while the user brings the target package or repository they want to create a skill for. ## KEY FINDINGS

## Comprehensive Analysis: SKF Tool Integration & Implementation Patterns

Based on my thorough code review, here are the answers to your questions:

---

## 1. **Are These Actual Code Implementations or Just Workflow Instructions?**

**Answer: Workflow instructions with CLI tool wrappers — not standalone code implementations.**

The tools are **NOT implemented in this repo**. Instead, SKF acts as an orchestration layer that:

- **Detects and wraps external tools** via CLI invocations in step files
- **Calls tools imperatively** from markdown step instructions
- **Parses tool output** back into context for further compilation

The "bridges" (`gh_bridge`, `ast_bridge`, `qmd_bridge`) are **naming conventions** used in step files to refer to tool invocations, not actual software modules. The term "bridge" means "wrapper around an external tool's CLI."

---

## 2. **How Each Tool Gets Invoked in Practice**

### **Quick Tier Tools (All Tiers)**

| Tool | Invocation | Purpose |
|------|-----------|---------|
| **GitHub CLI** | `gh --version` (detect)<br>`gh issue list`, `gh pr list`, `gh api` | Source reading, issue/PR mining |
| **skill-check** | `npx skill-check -h` (detect)<br>`npx skill-check check {dir} --format json --fix` | Validation + auto-fix<br>`npx skill-check split-body {dir} --write` | Body size splitting |
| **tessl** | `npx -y tessl --version` (detect)<br>`npx -y tessl skill review {dir}` | Content quality scoring |

### **Forge Tier Tools**

| Tool | Invocation | Purpose |
|------|-----------|---------|
| **ast-grep** | `ast-grep --version` (detect)<br>`ast-grep scan {path} ...` (inferred) | AST-based structural extraction |

### **Deep Tier Tools**

| Tool | Invocation | Purpose |
|------|-----------|---------|
| **QMD** | `qmd status` (detect)<br>`qmd collection add ...`<br>`qmd collection remove ...` | Knowledge indexing & search |
| **git** | `git --version` (detect)<br>`git clone --depth 1 ...` | Ephemeral source cloning |

### **Example: Step 04b — External Validators**

From `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md`:

```markdown
### 2. Run skill-check

Check availability:
npx skill-check -h

If unavailable, record `skill_check_score: N/A` and skip to section 3.

Run validation:
npx skill-check check {skillDir} --format json --no-security-scan

Parse JSON output to extract qualityScore, diagnostics[], errorCount, warningCount
Store in context: skill_check_score, skill_check_diagnostics

### 3. Run tessl

Check availability:
npx -y tessl --version

If unavailable, record `tessl_score: N/A` and skip to section 4.

Run review:
npx -y tessl skill review {skillDir}

Parse the output to extract description_score, content_score, average_score, validation_result, judge_suggestions[]
```

**Key Pattern:** Tools are invoked via `npx` (npm package runner), outputs parsed as JSON or text, results stored in context for subsequent steps.

---

## 3. **How Setup-Forge Detects Tools**

From `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-01-detect-and-tier.md`:

**Tier Detection Sequence:**

```
1. Verify Tool: ast-grep
   Run: ast-grep --version
   If succeeds: record {ast_grep: true}
   If fails: record {ast_grep: false}

2. Verify Tool: gh
   Run: gh --version
   If succeeds: record {gh_cli: true}
   If fails: record {gh_cli: false}

3. Verify Tool: qmd
   Run: qmd status
   If succeeds and indicates operational: record {qmd: true}
   If fails: record {qmd: false}

4. Calculate Tier (tier-rules.md):
   {ast_grep} AND {gh_cli} AND {qmd} all true → Deep
   {ast_grep} true (regardless of gh/qmd) → Forge
   Otherwise → Quick
```

**Critical:** Uses **verification commands**, not existence checks (`which`, `command -v`). A tool must be **functional**, not just on PATH.

---

## 4. **Where doc_fetcher Would Be Consumed**

Found references in:
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/docs/architecture.md` (line 246)
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` (line 67)

### **Current Status: PLANNED, NOT IMPLEMENTED**

From `docs/architecture.md`:

```
Planned (not yet implemented): **`doc_fetcher`** for remote documentation (Firecrawl/Jina.ai). 
Output will be quarantined as T3.
```

### **Intended Use Case (Scenario D: Docs-Only)**

From `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/docs/examples.md`:

```markdown
### Scenario D: Docs-Only (SaaS/Closed Source)

No source code available — only documentation.

Generate from docs + QMD-indexed content. T2/T3 confidence only. `source_authority: community`.
```

### **Where It Should Fit in Pipeline**

**Most likely location: Step 3 (Extract)** — after source extraction fails or for docs-only sources:

```markdown
# From step-03-extract.md:

**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
If source_repo is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format)...
If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):
  ⚠️ Degrade to source reading (T1-low)...
```

**For Docs-Only:** Would need a new step between step-02 (ecosystem-check) and step-03 (extract) that:
1. Detects when source is unavailable or docs-only
2. Invokes `doc_fetcher` to fetch remote docs (Firecrawl/Jina.ai)
3. Indexes into QMD as a temporary collection
4. Proceeds with extraction from QMD results (T3 confidence)

---

## 5. **How T3 Content Is Created and Quarantined**

### **Current Status: T3 Is THEORETICAL**

No existing step produces T3 content yet. However, the **citation format** is defined:

From `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md`:

```markdown
| Tier | Format | Example |
|------|--------|---------|
| T1 (AST) | `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` | `[AST:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
| T1-low (Source) | `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]` | `[SRC:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
| T2 (QMD) | `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]` | `[QMD:project:CHANGELOG.md]` |
| T3 (External) | `[EXT:{url}]` | `[EXT:docs.example.com/api]` |
```

### **Planned T3 Quarantine Strategy**

From `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/docs/architecture.md`:

```markdown
| Tier | Source | Tool | What It Means |
|------|--------|------|---------------|
| **T3** | External documentation | `doc_fetcher` | External, untrusted. Quarantined. |
```

**Confidence Constraint:**

```markdown
| Forge Tier | AST? | QMD? | Max Authority | Accuracy Guarantee |
|-----------|------|------|---------------|-------------------|
| Quick | No | No | `community` | Best-effort |
| Forge | Yes | No | `official` | Structural (AST-verified) |
| Deep | Yes | Yes | `official` | Full (structural + contextual + temporal) |
```

**Implication:** T3 content would force `source_authority: "community"` and never allow `source_authority: "official"`, regardless of tier.

---

## 6. **Firecrawl & Jina.ai Reference Patterns**

### **No Existing Integration**

No references to Firecrawl or Jina.ai CLI patterns found in the codebase. However, based on SKF's tool integration patterns:

### **Expected doc_fetcher Implementation Pattern**

Following SKF's established pattern, `doc_fetcher` would likely:

1. **Detect availability:**
   ```bash
   npx @firecrawl/firecrawl --version
   # OR
   curl -s https://api.jina.ai/health
   ```

2. **Invoke on a URL:**
   ```bash
   npx @firecrawl/firecrawl scrape https://docs.example.com/api --format markdown
   # OR
   curl -X POST https://api.jina.ai/extract -H "Authorization: Bearer {key}" -d '{"url": "..."}'
   ```

3. **Parse output** (likely markdown or JSON) into a temporary staging directory

4. **Index into QMD:**
   ```bash
   qmd collection add {staging-dir}/ --name {skill-name}-docs --mask "*.md"
   ```

5. **Register in forge-tier.yaml:**
   ```yaml
   - name: "{skill-name}-docs"
     type: "external"
     source_workflow: "create-skill"
     skill_name: "{skill-name}"
     created_at: "{timestamp}"
   ```

6. **Mark as T3 in SKILL.md citations:**
   ```markdown
   `getConfig()` — Returns app configuration object. [EXT:docs.example.com/api#getConfig]
   ```

---

## 7. **Full Tool Integration Pattern Summary**

### **The 5-Tool Ecosystem (Current)**

| Tier | Tool | Role | CLI Pattern | Status |
|------|------|------|-------------|--------|
| All | **skill-check** | Validation + scoring | `npx skill-check check --format json` | ✅ Implemented |
| All | **tessl** | Content quality | `npx -y tessl skill review` | ✅ Implemented |
| All | **GitHub CLI** | Source reading | `gh issue list`, `gh api` | ✅ Implemented |
| Forge+ | **ast-grep** | AST extraction | `ast-grep --version` | ✅ Implemented |
| Deep | **QMD** | Knowledge indexing | `qmd collection add` | ✅ Implemented |
| **PLANNED** | **doc_fetcher** | Remote docs | `npx @firecrawl/firecrawl scrape` | ⏳ Not implemented |

### **Integration Principles**

1. **Empirical verification:** Tools verified by running actual commands (`--version`, `status`), not existence checks
2. **Graceful degradation:** Tool unavailability → feature skip, never workflow halt
3. **CLI wrapping:** Tools invoked via standard subprocess (npm/bash), not embedded libraries
4. **Output parsing:** Results captured as JSON (preferred) or plaintext, stored in context
5. **Progressive disclosure:** Tool availability unlocks tiers (Quick → Forge → Deep)
6. **Confidence layering:** Each tool adds a confidence tier (T1 AST, T2 QMD, T3 docs)

---

## Implementation Plan: `doc_fetcher` for SKF

### Overview

`doc_fetcher` is the last unimplemented tool in the SKF tool ecosystem. It fetches remote documentation from URLs and produces T3-confidence content for skills. Its primary use case is Scenario D (docs-only / SaaS / closed-source), where no source code is available and documentation URLs are the only input. However, it is also useful at any tier as supplemental T3 content.

---

### Design Decision 1: Which CLI tool to use?

**Answer: Jina Reader as primary, Firecrawl as optional upgrade, with `curl` as implicit dependency.**

Rationale:
- Jina Reader requires only `curl` (effectively universally available) -- no installation needed: `curl -s https://r.jina.ai/{url}`
- Firecrawl requires `npx firecrawl` or an API key and npm package -- heavier dependency
- Following the progressive capability pattern, Jina should be the "always available" option (like `gh_bridge` for Quick tier), and Firecrawl should be optional if detected
- The detection pattern in step-01-detect-and-tier.md verifies tools empirically. `curl --version` is the verification for Jina Reader (always true). Firecrawl verification would be `npx firecrawl --help` or similar.
- Since `curl` is effectively guaranteed, `doc_fetcher` via Jina Reader should be treated as **always available** rather than a detected tool. This means it does NOT affect tier calculation.
- Firecrawl, if detected, could produce higher quality extraction (structured data, better pagination), but this is a future enhancement. Initial implementation should use Jina Reader only.

**Detection command:** `curl --version` (for Jina Reader availability)

**Invocation pattern:**
```bash
curl -s "https://r.jina.ai/{encoded_url}" -H "Accept: text/markdown"
```

This returns the URL content as clean markdown, which can then be parsed for API information.

---

### Design Decision 2: Where in the create-skill pipeline does doc fetching happen?

**Answer: New step file `step-03c-fetch-docs.md` inserted between step-03b (fetch-temporal) and step-04 (enrich).**

Rationale:
- The extraction step (step-03) focuses on source code extraction with T1/T1-low confidence
- The temporal fetch step (step-03b) fetches temporal context (issues, PRs) with T2 confidence
- Doc fetching produces T3 content from external URLs -- it logically follows source extraction and temporal fetching
- It must happen before step-04 (enrich) so that doc-fetched content is available for the enrichment phase to annotate
- The naming pattern `step-03c` follows the existing `step-03b` convention
- The step is always available (not tier-gated), but the content it produces is always T3
- For Scenario D (docs-only), step-03 would produce zero extractions (no source code), and step-03c would be the primary content producer

**Pipeline flow becomes:**
```
step-03-extract.md (T1/T1-low from source)
  -> step-03b-fetch-temporal.md (T2 temporal, Deep only)
    -> step-03c-fetch-docs.md (T3 from doc URLs, all tiers)
      -> step-04-enrich.md (QMD enrichment, Deep only)
```

---

### Design Decision 3: Does doc_fetcher affect tier calculation?

**Answer: No. `doc_fetcher` is tier-independent -- available at all tiers.**

Rationale:
- The existing tier-rules.md defines tiers based on ast-grep, gh, and qmd. Doc fetching uses `curl`, which is universally available.
- T3 content has the lowest priority in conflict resolution. Adding it never elevates or changes the capability tier.
- The tool availability could optionally be recorded in forge-tier.yaml under a new section (e.g., `doc_fetcher: true`), but it does NOT participate in tier calculation.
- This aligns with how skill-check and tessl work -- they are available tools that don't affect tiering.

---

### Design Decision 4: How does the brief indicate docs-only mode?

**Answer: New optional field `doc_urls` in skill-brief.yaml, plus a `source_type` discriminator.**

The skill-brief schema needs extension:

```yaml
# New optional fields
source_type: "source" | "docs-only"   # default: "source"
doc_urls:
  - url: "https://docs.example.com/api"
    label: "API Reference"
  - url: "https://docs.example.com/getting-started"
    label: "Getting Started"
```

When `source_type: "docs-only"`:
- `source_repo` becomes optional (or can point to a documentation site URL for reference)
- `doc_urls` becomes required (at least one URL)
- Step-03 (extract) is effectively skipped (no source code to extract)
- Step-03c (fetch-docs) becomes the primary content producer
- `source_authority` is forced to `community` (T3 cannot produce `official`)
- All content gets `[EXT:{url}]` citations

When `source_type: "source"` (default, backward compatible):
- Existing behavior unchanged
- `doc_urls` is optional -- if present, doc_fetcher supplements extraction with T3 content
- T3 content never overrides T1/T1-low/T2 content (lowest priority in conflict resolution)

---

#### Files to CREATE (5 files):

1. **`src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md`** -- New step file for doc fetching (primary deliverable)

2. **`src/knowledge/doc-fetcher.md`** -- New knowledge fragment documenting the doc_fetcher tool, its T3 quarantine rules, and usage patterns

3. *(No other new files needed -- the tool is integrated into existing workflow structures)*

#### Phase 3: Core Implementation (step-03c-fetch-docs.md)

**Step 3.1: Create the new step file**

Create `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md`:

The step file should follow the exact structure of step-03b-fetch-temporal.md (the closest analog). Key sections:

**Frontmatter:**
```yaml
---
name: 'step-03c-fetch-docs'
description: 'Fetch remote documentation from URLs for T3-confidence content — all tiers'
nextStepFile: './step-04-enrich.md'
---
```

**Step Goal:** Fetch remote documentation from brief-specified URLs using doc_fetcher (Jina Reader via curl), extract API information, and add T3-confidence content to the extraction inventory.

**Mandatory Sequence:**

1. **Check Eligibility**
   - Check if `doc_urls` is present in the brief (either from `source_type: "docs-only"` or as supplemental URLs in a standard brief)
   - If no `doc_urls` specified: auto-proceed silently to step-04 (same pattern as step-03b for non-Deep tiers)
   - Check `doc_fetcher` availability from forge-tier.yaml tools section
   - If `doc_fetcher: false` (curl unavailable): warn and skip

2. **Security Notice**
   - Following the architecture.md requirement: "doc_fetcher warns before transmitting URLs to external services"
   - Display: "**External fetch notice:** The following URLs will be sent to r.jina.ai for content extraction: {list URLs}. Jina Reader converts web pages to markdown. No source code is transmitted."
   - For `source_type: "docs-only"` mode: auto-proceed (user already specified URLs in the brief, implicit consent)
   - For supplemental doc_urls: this is informational only, no gate needed (the brief was already approved with these URLs)

3. **Fetch Documentation**
   - For each URL in `doc_urls`:
     ```bash
     curl -s "https://r.jina.ai/{encoded_url}" -H "Accept: text/markdown"
     ```
   - If fetch succeeds: store the markdown content with the URL as provenance
   - If fetch fails (network error, 404, timeout): log warning, skip that URL, continue with remaining URLs
   - Rate limiting: 1-second delay between fetches to respect Jina Reader rate limits
   - Timeout: 30 seconds per URL

4. **Extract API Information from Fetched Content**
   - Parse the fetched markdown for:
     - Function/method signatures
     - Parameter descriptions
     - Return types
     - Configuration options
     - Usage examples (from the documentation)
   - All extracted items get T3 confidence: `[EXT:{url}]` citation
   - `source_authority: community` is enforced

5. **Build Doc-Fetch Inventory**
   - For `source_type: "docs-only"`: this IS the extraction inventory (replaces the empty inventory from step-03)
   - For supplemental mode: merge T3 items into existing extraction inventory
   - T3 items never override existing T1/T1-low/T2 items for the same export
   - Aggregate counts: URLs fetched, URLs failed, T3 items extracted

6. **Report**
   - Display brief summary:
     "**Documentation fetched.**
     **URLs processed:** {fetched}/{total}
     **T3 items extracted:** {count}
     **Confidence:** All items T3 — `[EXT:{url}]` citations
     Proceeding to enrichment..."
   - For docs-only mode, adjust: "**Docs-only mode:** All content is T3 (external documentation). `source_authority: community`."

7. **Auto-Proceed** to step-04 (enrich)

**Graceful degradation rules (following existing pattern):**
- curl unavailable: skip with warning
- Individual URL fetch failure: skip that URL, continue
- All URLs fail: warn, proceed with empty T3 inventory (extraction from step-03 still available if source mode)
- Empty doc content: skip, continue
- Never halt the workflow

**Step 3.2: Update step-03b nextStepFile**

Modify `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`:

Modify `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`:

---

### Potential Challenges and Mitigations

1. **Jina Reader rate limiting**: The implementation should include a 1-second delay between requests and handle HTTP 429 gracefully (stop fetching, keep results so far -- same pattern as targeted GitHub searches in step-03b section 3b).

2. **Large documentation pages**: Jina Reader may return very large markdown documents. The step should truncate to a reasonable size (e.g., first 10,000 lines) and warn if truncation occurred.

3. **Doc URL content quality varies wildly**: Some URLs will return useful API docs, others will return marketing pages. The step should focus on extracting structured API information (function signatures, parameters) rather than prose. Content that cannot produce structured API items should be noted as "low-extraction-yield" but not discarded.

4. **Docs-only mode interaction with Gate 2**: In step-03, Gate 2 presents extraction results for user confirmation. For docs-only mode, step-03 would produce zero extractions. Two options: (a) skip Gate 2 for docs-only and defer to step-03c, or (b) show "0 exports -- docs-only mode, documentation will be fetched next." Option (b) is safer and more transparent. Step-03c could then have its own confirmation gate showing what was fetched from docs.

5. **Interaction with step-04 (enrich)**: QMD enrichment in step-04 operates on the extraction inventory. For docs-only mode, T3 items from doc_fetcher become the inventory. QMD enrichment can still add T2 annotations to T3 items (if QMD collections exist). This is fine -- T2 annotations are additive and can supplement T3 content.

6. **Backward compatibility**: The `source_type` field defaults to `"source"`, and `doc_urls` is optional. Existing briefs without these fields continue to work exactly as before. No migration needed.

---

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/plans/gleaming-dancing-matsumoto.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Implement doc_fetcher — T3 external documentation support\n\n## Context\n\n`doc_fetcher` is the last unimplemented tool in SKF's ecosystem. It's documented in `docs/architecture.md` as \"Planned (not yet implemented)\" and referenced in `docs/examples.md` Scenario D (Docs-Only/SaaS/Closed Source). All T3 confidence tier infrastructure is already in place (`[EXT:{url}]` citation format, `confidence_t3` in metadata schema, T3 rows in knowledge fragments) — but no workflow step produces T3 content. This blocks the docs-only use case entirely.\n\n**Goal:** Implement doc_fetcher as a **tool-agnostic** step that instructs the agent to fetch URLs using whatever web fetching capability is available in its environment (Firecrawl MCP, WebFetch, curl, browser tools, etc.). Zero breaking changes — all new fields are optional with backward-compatible defaults.\n\n## Key Design Decision: Tool-Agnostic Fetching\n\nUnlike gh_bridge or ast_bridge which wrap specific CLI tools, `doc_fetcher` is **environment-dependent**. The LLM agent executing the workflow may have:\n- Firecrawl MCP server\n- Built-in WebFetch tool\n- curl access via Bash\n- Browser automation tools\n- Other web fetching capabilities\n\nThe step file instructs the agent to fetch URLs and extract content — the agent uses whatever capability is available in its environment. This follows the \"tools are backstage\" principle from the agent persona.\n\n**Detection:** Instead of checking for a specific CLI tool, the step self-detects by attempting a test fetch. If the agent cannot fetch any URL, it skips gracefully.\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Phase 1: Brief Schema Extension (3 files)\n\n**1a. `src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md`**\n- Add optional field `source_type`: enum `source` | `docs-only`, default `source`\n- Add optional field `doc_urls`: array of `{url, label}` objects\n- When `source_type: \"docs-only\"`: `source_repo` becomes optional, `doc_urls` required (>= 1), `source_authority` forced to `community`\n- Update YAML template and validation rules\n\n**1b. `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`**\n- In target gathering, add third option: \"Documentation URLs (docs-only mode)\"\n- When user provides doc URLs: set `source_type: \"docs-only\"`, collect URLs with labels\n\n**1c. `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md`**\n- Add conditional for `source_type: \"docs-only\"`: scope by doc page selection rather than file patterns\n\n### Phase 2: Core Step File (1 new file + 2 modifications)\n\n**2a. CREATE `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md`** (~180 lines)\n\nFollows step-03b pattern. Key design:\n\n```\nFrontmatter:\n  name: step-03c-fetch-docs\n  description: Fetch remote documentation from URLs for T3-confidence content\n  nextStepFile: ./step-04-enrich.md\n\nSequence:\n  1. Check Eligibility\n     - Skip if no doc_urls in brief (auto-proceed silently)\n     - This step is available at ALL tiers (not tier-gated)\n\n  2. Security Notice\n     - \"The following documentation URLs will be fetched: {list}\"\n     - Informational only, not blocking (user already approved URLs in the brief)\n\n  3. Fetch Documentation (tool-agnostic)\n     - For each URL in doc_urls:\n       \"Fetch the content at {url} as markdown. Use whatever web fetching\n       capability is available in your environment (Firecrawl, WebFetch,\n       web-reader, curl, etc.).\"\n     - Individual fetch failures = skip that URL, log warning, continue\n     - Never halt on fetch failure\n\n  4. Extract API Information from Fetched Content\n     - Parse fetched markdown for: function signatures, parameters, return\n       types, configuration options, usage examples\n     - All extracted items get T3 confidence: [EXT:{url}] citation\n     - source_authority forced to \"community\" for docs-only mode\n\n  5. Build Doc-Fetch Inventory\n     - Docs-only mode: this IS the extraction inventory (replaces empty step-03)\n     - Supplemental mode: merge T3 items into existing inventory\n     - T3 never overrides T1/T1-low/T2 items (lowest conflict priority)\n\n  6. Report — URLs processed, T3 items extracted\n  7. Auto-Proceed to step-04\n```\n\n**2b. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`**\n- Change `nextStepFile` from `./step-04-enrich.md` to `./step-03c-fetch-docs.md`\n\n**2c. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`**\n- Add early-exit for `source_type: \"docs-only\"`: skip extraction, build empty inventory, display \"Docs-only mode: documentation will be fetched in step-03c\"\n\n### Phase 3: Compilation Adaptation (1 file)\n\n**3a. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md`**\n- Add conditional for docs-only mode:\n  - Overview notes \"Generated from external documentation\"\n  - metadata.json `source_authority` forced to `community`\n  - All citations use `[EXT:...]` format\n\n### Phase 4: Knowledge & Docs (4 files)\n\n**4a. ## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Implement doc_fetcher — T3 external documentation support

## Context

`doc_fetcher` is the last unimplemented tool in SKF's ecosystem. It's documented in `docs/architecture.md` as "Planned (not yet implemented)" and referenced in `docs/examples.md` Scenario D (Docs-Only/SaaS/Closed Source). All T3 confidence tier infrastructure is already in place (`[EXT:{url}]` citation format, `confidence_t3` in metadata schema, T3 rows in knowledge fragments) — but no workflow step produces T3 content. This blocks the docs-only use case entirely.

**Goal:** Implement doc_fetcher as a **tool-agnostic** step that instructs the agent to fetch URLs using whatever web fetching capability is available in its environment (Firecrawl MCP, WebFetch, curl, browser tools, etc.). Zero breaking changes — all new fields are optional with backward-compatible defaults.

## Key Design Decision: Tool-Agnostic Fetching

Unlike gh_bridge or ast_bridge which wrap specific CLI tools, `doc_fetcher` is **environment-dependent**. The LLM agent executing the workflow may have:
- Firecrawl MCP server
- Built-in WebFetch tool
- curl access via Bash
- Browser automation tools
- Other web fetching capabilities

The step file instructs the agent to fetch URLs and extract content — the agent uses whatever capability is available in its environment. This follows the "tools are backstage" principle from the agent persona.

**Detection:** Instead of checking for a specific CLI tool, the step self-detects by attempting a test fetch. If the agent cannot fetch any URL, it skips gracefully.

## Implementation

### Phase 1: Brief Schema Extension (3 files)

**1a. `src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md`**
- Add optional field `source_type`: enum `source` | `docs-only`, default `source`
- Add optional field `doc_urls`: array of `{url, label}` objects
- When `source_type: "docs-only"`: `source_repo` becomes optional, `doc_urls` required (>= 1), `source_authority` forced to `community`
- Update YAML template and validation rules

**1b. `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`**
- In target gathering, add third option: "Documentation URLs (docs-only mode)"
- When user provides doc URLs: set `source_type: "docs-only"`, collect URLs with labels

**1c. `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md`**
- Add conditional for `source_type: "docs-only"`: scope by doc page selection rather than file patterns

### Phase 2: Core Step File (1 new file + 2 modifications)

**2a. CREATE `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md`** (~180 lines)

Follows step-03b pattern. Key design:

```
Frontmatter:
  name: step-03c-fetch-docs
  description: Fetch remote documentation from URLs for T3-confidence content
  nextStepFile: ./step-04-enrich.md

Sequence:
  1. Check Eligibility
     - Skip if no doc_urls in brief (auto-proceed silently)
     - This step is available at ALL tiers (not tier-gated)

  2. Security Notice
     - "The following documentation URLs will be fetched: {list}"
     - Informational only, not blocking (user already approved URLs in the brief)

  3. Fetch Documentation (tool-agnostic)
     - For each URL in doc_urls:
       "Fetch the content at {url} as markdown. Use whatever web fetching
       capability is available in your environment (Firecrawl, WebFetch,
       web-reader, curl, etc.)."
     - Individual fetch failures = skip that URL, log warning, continue
     - Never halt on fetch failure

  4. Extract API Information from Fetched Content
     - Parse fetched markdown for: function signatures, parameters, return
       types, configuration options, usage examples
     - All extracted items get T3 confidence: [EXT:{url}] citation
     - source_authority forced to "community" for docs-only mode

  5. Build Doc-Fetch Inventory
     - Docs-only mode: this IS the extraction inventory (replaces empty step-03)
     - Supplemental mode: merge T3 items into existing inventory
     - T3 never overrides T1/T1-low/T2 items (lowest conflict priority)

  6. Report — URLs processed, T3 items extracted
  7. Auto-Proceed to step-04
```

**2b. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md`**
- Change `nextStepFile` from `./step-04-enrich.md` to `./step-03c-fetch-docs.md`

**2c. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`**
- Add early-exit for `source_type: "docs-only"`: skip extraction, build empty inventory, display "Docs-only mode: documentation will be fetched in step-03c"

### Phase 3: Compilation Adaptation (1 file)

**3a. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md`**
- Add conditional for docs-only mode:
  - Overview notes "Generated from external documentation"
  - metadata.json `source_authority` forced to `community`
  - All citations use `[EXT:...]` format

### Phase 4: Knowledge & Docs (4 files)

**4a. CREATE `src/knowledge/doc-fetcher.md`** (~60 lines)
- T3 quarantine rules, tool-agnostic fetching principle, conflict resolution, security
- Follow existing fragment structure

**4b. `src/knowledge/skf-knowledge-index.csv`**
- Add row for doc-fetcher fragment (10th entry)

**4c. `docs/architecture.md`**
- Line 246: Remove "Planned (not yet implemented)", describe as tool-agnostic fetcher
- Update tool table entry: `doc_fetcher | Environment web tools | Remote documentation fetching, T3-confidence content extraction`
- Update "5 Tools" header to "6 Tools" (or keep as-is since doc_fetcher is tool-agnostic)

**4d. `docs/examples.md`**
- Expand Scenario D with concrete brief example showing `source_type: "docs-only"` and `doc_urls`

### Phase 5: Config (no tier detection needed)

Since doc_fetcher is tool-agnostic (uses whatever the environment provides), it does NOT need:
- ~~Detection in setup-forge step-01~~ — no specific tool to detect
- ~~`doc_fetcher` field in forge-tier.yaml~~ — not a detected tool
- ~~Tier rule changes~~ — doesn't affect tier calculation

The step self-gates on `doc_urls` presence in the brief. If URLs exist, it fetches. If the agent can't fetch (no web capability), the step degrades gracefully per existing pattern.

## Files Summary

| Action | File |
|--------|------|
| CREATE | `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md` |
| CREATE | `src/knowledge/doc-fetcher.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/workflows/brief-skill/data/skill-brief-schema.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/workflows/brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md` |
| MODIFY | `src/knowledge/skf-knowledge-index.csv` |
| MODIFY | `docs/architecture.md` |
| MODIFY | `docs/examples.md` |

**2 new files, 9 modified files**

## Backward Compatibility

- `source_type` defaults to `"source"` — existing briefs unchanged
- `doc_urls` is optional — existing briefs without it skip step-03c silently
- No tier detection changes — no forge-tier.yaml changes needed
- All new step logic gates on `doc_urls` presence — no-op when absent

## Verification

1. `npm test` — all existing tests pass
2. `grep -r "step-03c" src/workflows/create-skill/` — chain: step-03b → step-03c → step-04
3. `wc -l src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md` — ≤ 250 lines
4. Knowledge index CSV has 10 entries (was 9)
5. `docs/architecture.md` no longer says "Planned (not yet implemented)"
6. Markdown lint + format check pass

Key differences:\n- This step is available at ALL tiers (not tier-gated like step-03b which is Deep only)\n- Tool-agnostic: agent uses whatever web fetching is available\n- Graceful degradation: if no web fetching available, skip with warning\n\n### MANDATORY SEQUENCE (7 sections):\n\n1. **Check Eligibility**\n   - Check if `doc_urls` is present in the brief data (from step-01 context)\n   - If no `doc_urls` specified: auto-proceed silently to {nextStepFile} (same pattern as step-03b's tier skip)\n   - No tier gate — this step runs at any tier when doc_urls are present\n\n2. **Security Notice**\n   - \"The following documentation URLs will be fetched externally: {list each URL with label}\"\n   - \"Content fetched from external URLs is classified as T3 (external, untrusted) and cited as `[EXT:{url}]`.\"\n   - Informational only — not a gate (user already approved URLs in the brief)\n\n3. **Fetch Documentation**\n   - For each URL in `doc_urls`:\n     - \"Fetch the content at `{url}` as clean markdown. Use whatever web fetching capability is available in your environment (e.g., Firecrawl, WebFetch, web-reader, curl, or browser tools).\"\n     - If fetch succeeds: store the markdown content with the URL as provenance source\n     - If fetch fails: log warning \"Failed to fetch {url}: {reason}. Skipping.\", continue with remaining URLs\n   - If ALL URLs fail: report warning \"No documentation could be fetched. Proceeding without T3 content.\", auto-proceed\n\n4. **Extract API Information from Fetched Content**\n   - Parse fetched markdown for:\n     - Function/method signatures and their parameters\n     - Return types and data structures\n     - Configuration options and their defaults\n     - Usage examples and code snippets\n   - Every extracted item gets T3 confidence citation: `[EXT:{url}]`\n   - Do NOT hallucinate — if information cannot be found in the fetched content, exclude it\n\n5. **Build Doc-Fetch Inventory**\n   - If `source_type: \"docs-only\"` in brief: the doc-fetch inventory IS the extraction inventory (replaces the empty inventory from step-03)\n   - If `source_type: \"source\"` (supplemental mode): merge T3 items into the existing extraction inventory from step-03\n   - **Conflict rule:** T3 items NEVER override existing T1, T1-low, or T2 items for the same export. T3 has lowest priority.\n   - Aggregate: URLs fetched successfully, URLs failed, T3 items extracted\n\n6. **Report**\n   - \"**Documentation fetch complete.**\n     **URLs processed:** {fetched}/{total}\n     **T3 items extracted:** {count}\n     **Confidence:** All doc-fetched items are T3 — `[EXT:{url}]` citations\n     {If docs-only mode: '**Mode:** Docs-only — all skill content is T3. source_authority: community'}\n     Proceeding to enrichment...\"\n\n7. **Auto-Proceed**\n   - After doc fetch is complete (or skipped), immediately load {nextStepFile}\n   - This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices\n\n### Success/Failure Metrics:\nFollow the exact same pattern as step-03b.\n\nSUCCESS:\n- No doc_urls → skipped silently, auto-proceeded\n- doc_urls present → each URL fetched using available web tools\n- Individual fetch failures handled gracefully (skip and continue)\n- All content cited as T3 with [EXT:{url}] provenance\n- Existing T1/T1-low/T2 items never overridden by T3\n- Auto-proceeded to step-04\n\nFAILURE:\n- Halting because web fetching is unavailable\n- Including fetched content without [EXT:{url}] citations\n- Overriding existing higher-confidence extractions with T3 data\n- Hardcoding a specific fetching tool instead of being tool-agnostic\n- Beginning compilation in this step (that's step-05)\n\nIMPORTANT: Keep the file under 250 lines. Be concise but complete. After creating the file, verify the line count with `wc -l`."
}
```

   234→## Tool Ecosystem
   235→
   236→### 5 Tools
   237→
   238→| Tool | Wraps | Purpose |
   239→|------|-------|---------|
   240→| **`gh_bridge`** | GitHub CLI (`gh`) | Source code access, issue mining, release tracking, PR intelligence |
   241→| **`skill-check`** | [thedaviddias/skill-check](https://github.com/thedaviddias/skill-check) | Validation + auto-fix (`check --fix`), quality scoring (0-100), security scan, split-body, diff comparison |
   242→| **`tessl`** | [tessl](https://tessl.io) | Content quality review, actionability scoring, progressive disclosure evaluation, AI judge with suggestions |
   243→| **`ast_bridge`** | ast-grep CLI | Structural extraction, custom AST queries, co-import detection |
   244→| **`qmd_bridge`** | QMD (local search) | BM25 keyword search, vector semantic search, collection indexing |
   245→
   246→Planned (not yet implemented): **`doc_fetcher`** for remote documentation (Firecrawl/Jina.ai). Output will be quarantined as T3.
   247→
   248→### Conflict Resolution
   249→
   250→When tools disagree, higher priority wins for instructions. Lower priority is preserved as annotations:
   251→
   252→| Priority | Source | Tool |
   253→|----------|--------|------|
   254→| 1 (highest) | AST extraction | `ast_bridge` |
   255→| 2 | QMD evidence | `qmd_bridge` |
   256→| 3 | Source reading (non-AST) | `gh_bridge` |
   257→| 4 | External documentation | `doc_fetcher` |
   258→
   259→### Internal Utility
   260→
   261→**`manifest_reader`** detects and parses dependency files across ecosystems:
   262→
   263→- **Full support:** `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `requirements.txt`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`

  "new_string": "### 6 Tools\n\n| Tool | Wraps | Purpose |\n|------|-------|---------|\n| **`gh_bridge`** | GitHub CLI (`gh`) | Source code access, issue mining, release tracking, PR intelligence |\n| **`skill-check`** | [thedaviddias/skill-check](https://github.com/thedaviddias/skill-check) | Validation + auto-fix (`check --fix`), quality scoring (0-100), security scan, split-body, diff comparison |\n| **`tessl`** | [tessl](https://tessl.io) | Content quality review, actionability scoring, progressive disclosure evaluation, AI judge with suggestions |\n| **`ast_bridge`** | ast-grep CLI | Structural extraction, custom AST queries, co-import detection |\n| **`qmd_bridge`** | QMD (local search) | BM25 keyword search, vector semantic search, collection indexing |\n| **`doc_fetcher`** | Environment web tools | Remote documentation fetching for T3-confidence content. Tool-agnostic — uses whatever web fetching is available (Firecrawl, WebFetch, curl, etc.). Output quarantined as T3. |"
}

Now I have all the information needed to answer your questions. Let me provide a comprehensive analysis based on what I've read:

## Analysis of QMD Collection Pipeline

### 1. Collection Types

Based on `qmd-registry.md`, **three collection types exist**:
- **`extraction`** — Compiled SKILL.md, references, context-snippet — structured, confidence-rated exports (producer: create-skill step-07; consumers: audit-skill for drift detection, update-skill for T2 enrichment)
- **`brief`** — skill-brief.yaml — intent, scope, target repository metadata (producer: brief-skill step-05; consumer: cross-skill deduplication at portfolio level)
- **`temporal`** — Issues, PRs, changelogs, release notes (producer: create-skill step-03b; consumer: step-04-enrich for T2 context annotations)

### 2. QMD Registration Format (step-03b → forge-tier.yaml)

Step-03b registers temporal collections **exactly** as:

```yaml
  - name: "{skill-name}-temporal"
    type: "temporal"
    source_workflow: "create-skill"
    skill_name: "{skill-name}"
    created_at: "{current ISO date}"
```

The registration is appended/replaced in the `qmd_collections` array in `forge-tier.yaml` (sidecar path: `_bmad/_memory/forger-sidecar/forge-tier.yaml`).

### 3. Step-04-enrich Collection Discovery & Filter

Step-04 discovers and filters collections using **collection type**:

1. Reads `qmd_collections` from sidecar `forge-tier.yaml`
2. **Type filter:** `type` **equal to** `"temporal"` (explicit requirement: "Do NOT include `"extraction"` or `"brief"` type collections")
3. If no temporal collections exist → reports and auto-proceeds gracefully (not an error)
4. If temporal collections exist → performs QMD searches scoped by module context for top-level public API functions only (10-20, not all exports)

The type filter is **strict by type equality**, not by presence:
```
if collection.type == "temporal":
    use for enrichment
else:
    skip
```

### 4. Doc-Fetched Content & QMD Benefit

**Yes, doc-fetched content would benefit from QMD indexing.** Here's the value proposition:

- Step-03c **currently does NOT index** fetched docs into QMD (this is the missing link)
- Step-04-enrich currently only searches `temporal` collections (issues/PRs/changelogs from step-03b)
- If step-03c were to index doc-fetched content into QMD, step-04 could:
  - Enrich source-extracted functions with **doc-fetched parameter signatures, return types, usage examples** (T3 confidence)
  - Cross-reference doc examples with extracted implementations
  - Provide more comprehensive context for enriched exports

The docs represent **external, curated documentation** — a higher-signal information source than raw temporal context.

### 5. Proposed Collection Type for Doc-Fetched Content

I would propose **`"docs"`** as the collection type. Rationale:

- **`"docs"`** is semantically clear and distinct from `"temporal"`, `"extraction"`, and `"brief"`
- Follows the registry pattern: `{skill-name}-docs`
- Signals "external, curated documentation" vs. temporal "repository activity"
- Allows step-04 to filter: `type == "docs"` for documentation searches (or `type in ["temporal", "docs"]` if enriching with both)

Alternative considered: `"external"` — too generic and doesn't distinguish between different doc sources.

### 6. Current Implementation Status (step-03c)

**Step-03c does NOT currently index into QMD.** This is a missing link:

- ✅ Step-03c fetches docs from URLs, extracts T3 items, merges into inventory
- ✅ Step-03c cites items as `[EXT:{url}]`
- ❌ Step-03c does **not** create a QMD collection for doc-fetched content
- ❌ Step-03c does **not** register in forge-tier.yaml

**Comparison to step-03b (temporal context):**
- Step-03b writes fetched content to staging dir → indexes via `qmd collection add` → registers in forge-tier.yaml → cleans up staging
- Step-03c writes T3 items **only to extraction inventory in context** — no QMD indexing or forge-tier registration

---

## Summary: The Missing QMD Link

**Current flow:**
```
step-03c (fetch docs)
  → T3 items merged into extraction_inventory in context
  → NO QMD indexing
  → step-04-enrich (searches only temporal collections)
  → step-05 (compile, doesn't search docs)
```

**Proposed flow to activate doc-fetched enrichment:**
```
step-03c (fetch docs)
  → T3 items merged into extraction_inventory in context
  → Index doc-fetched content into QMD collection type="docs"
  → Register {skill-name}-docs in forge-tier.yaml
  → Clean up staging files
  → step-04-enrich (searches temporal + docs collections)
  → step-05 (compile with full T2/T3 context)
```

The **exact registration format** for `step-03c` would mirror step-03b:

```yaml
  - name: "{skill-name}-docs"
    type: "docs"
    source_workflow: "create-skill"
    skill_name: "{skill-name}"
    created_at: "{current ISO date}"
```

And step-04's collection discovery would need to extend the type filter from `type == "temporal"` to `type in ["temporal", "docs"]`.

> AGENT

Yes — there's a clear missing link. Here's the natural connection:

**Current state:** step-03c fetches docs and puts T3 items into the extraction inventory in-memory. At Deep tier, this content is **not** indexed into QMD, so step-04 (enrich) can't cross-reference it.

**The natural Deep tier link:**
1. step-03c indexes fetched doc markdown into a QMD collection (type: `"docs"`)
2. step-03c registers `{skill-name}-docs` in forge-tier.yaml (same pattern as step-03b's temporal collections)
3. step-04 extends its collection filter from `type == "temporal"` to include `"docs"` collections too

This means at Deep tier, QMD can cross-reference doc-fetched content with source-extracted functions — enriching T1 exports with T3 doc context (usage examples, parameter details, deprecation notes from official docs).

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Add QMD indexing to doc_fetcher for Deep tier enrichment

## Context

step-03c-fetch-docs.md was just implemented but it only stores T3 items in the extraction inventory in-memory. At Deep tier, the fetched documentation markdown is not indexed into QMD, so step-04 (enrich) cannot cross-reference it with source-extracted functions. This breaks the natural Deep tier pipeline where QMD enables cross-referencing between data sources.

The existing pattern (step-03b temporal → QMD → step-04 enrichment) should extend to doc-fetched content: step-03c docs → QMD → step-04 enrichment.

## Changes (3 files)

### 1. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-fetch-docs.md`

After section 5 (Build Doc-Fetch Inventory), add a new section **5b. Index into QMD (Deep Tier Only)**:

- Gate: only execute at Deep tier (same pattern as step-03b)
- Write fetched markdown to a staging directory
- Index via: `qmd collection add {staging-dir}/ --name {skill-name}-docs --mask "*.md"`
- Register in forge-tier.yaml `qmd_collections`:
  ```yaml
  - name: "{skill-name}-docs"
    type: "docs"
    source_workflow: "create-skill"
    skill_name: "{skill-name}"
    created_at: "{current ISO date}"
  ```
- Clean up staging directory
- If QMD indexing fails: warn, continue (graceful degradation)

### 2. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-04-enrich.md`

In section 2 (Collection Inventory Pre-check), extend the type filter:

- **Current:** `type` equal to `"temporal"`
- **New:** `type` equal to `"temporal"` OR `"docs"`
- Update the "no temporal collections" message to: "no temporal or docs collections available"

### 3. `src/knowledge/qmd-registry.md`

Add `"docs"` to the collection types table:
- `docs` | External documentation (fetched URLs) | create-skill step-03c | step-04 enrichment

## Verification

1. `npm test` — all tests pass
2. grep step-03c for QMD registration code
3. grep step-04-enrich for updated type filter
4. Verify step-03c stays ≤ 250 lines after addition

   100→
   101→**Mode determines merge behavior:**
   102→
   103→- **`source_type: "docs-only"`** — The doc-fetch inventory IS the extraction inventory. It replaces the empty inventory from step-03, since there was no source code to extract from.
   104→- **`source_type: "source"` (supplemental mode)** — Merge T3 items into the existing extraction inventory from step-03.
   105→
   106→**Conflict rule:** T3 items NEVER override existing T1, T1-low, or T2 items for the same export. When an export already has a higher-confidence entry, the T3 item is discarded. T3 has the lowest priority.
   107→
   108→**Aggregate totals for reporting:**
   109→- URLs fetched successfully vs. total
   110→- URLs that failed
   111→- T3 items extracted
   112→
   113→### 6. Report
   114→
   115→Display:
   116→
   117→"**Documentation fetch complete.**
   118→**URLs processed:** {fetched}/{total}
   119→**T3 items extracted:** {count}
   120→**Confidence:** All doc-fetched items are T3 — `[EXT:{url}]` citations applied.
   121→{If docs-only mode: '**Mode:** Docs-only — all skill content is T3. source_authority: community'}
   122→
   123→Proceeding to enrichment..."
   124→
   125→### 7. Menu Handling Logic
   126→
   127→**Auto-proceed step — no user interaction.**
   128→
   129→After documentation fetch is complete (or skipped for any reason), immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
   130→
   131→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   132→
   133→- This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices
   134→- No `doc_urls` in brief: skip directly to next step with no output
   135→- No web fetching available: skip with warning then auto-proceed
   136→- All URLs failed: skip with warning then auto-proceed
   137→- Successful fetch: display report then auto-proceed
   138→- All failures degrade gracefully — skip and auto-proceed
   139→
   140→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   141→
   142→ONLY WHEN documentation is fetched and T3 items are merged into the extraction inventory (or the step is skipped due to no `doc_urls`, no web tools, or fetch failures) will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for enrichment.
   143→
   144→---
   145→
   146→## SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   147→
   148→### SUCCESS:
   149→
   150→- No `doc_urls` in brief: skipped silently, auto-proceeded
   151→- `doc_urls` present: each URL fetched using whatever web tool is available
   152→- Individual fetch failures handled gracefully (skip and continue)
   153→- All extracted content cited as T3 with `[EXT:{url}]` provenance
   154→- Existing T1/T1-low/T2 items never overridden by T3 data
   155→- Docs-only mode: doc-fetch inventory correctly replaces empty extraction inventory
   156→- Supplemental mode: T3 items merged into existing inventory respecting conflict rule
   157→- Auto-proceeded to step-04
   158→
   159→### SYSTEM FAILURE:
   160→
   161→- Halting the workflow because web fetching is unavailable or a URL fails
   162→- Including fetched content without `[EXT:{url}]` citations
   163→- Overriding existing higher-confidence extractions (T1, T1-low, T2) with T3 data
   164→- Hardcoding a specific fetching tool instead of being tool-agnostic
   165→- Hallucinating API details not found in the fetched content
   166→- Beginning compilation in this step (that is step-05)
   167→
   168→**Master Rule:** Documentation fetching is best-effort T3 enrichment. Fetch what you can, cite everything as `[EXT:{url}]`, never override higher-confidence data, and move on. Failures degrade gracefully — they never block the skill compilation pipeline.
   169→

```
     1→# QMD Collection Registry
     2→
     3→## Principle
     4→
     5→QMD collections in the Skill Forge follow a **progressive registry architecture**: workflow steps that produce artifacts index them into QMD collections and register them in `forge-tier.yaml`. Downstream workflow steps discover and query collections through the registry — never by convention or assumption. Collections are created by producers, consumed by searchers, and maintained by a janitor.
     6→
     7→## Rationale
     8→
     9→Early SKF versions used a blind auto-index strategy: setup-forge would scan all project directories and index them into QMD collections. This created three problems:
    10→
    11→- On fresh workspaces, auto-indexing found nothing — wasted work
    12→- Raw source code indexing produced low signal-to-noise search results
    13→- No connection between what was indexed and what workflows actually queried
    14→
    15→The progressive registry solves all three by indexing **curated workflow artifacts** (extraction snapshots, skill briefs) instead of raw source files, and only when a workflow produces them.
    16→
    17→## Architecture
    18→
    19→### Roles
    20→
    21→| Role | Workflow | Responsibility |
    22→| --- | --- | --- |
    23→| **Producer** | brief-skill, create-skill | Creates QMD collections from workflow artifacts and registers them in forge-tier.yaml |
    24→| **Consumer** | audit-skill, update-skill | Reads the registry, discovers collections by skill name and type, queries via qmd_bridge |
    25→| **Janitor** | setup-forge | Cross-references live QMD collections against the registry, cleans orphans and stale entries |
    26→
    27→### Registry Schema
    28→
    29→The `qmd_collections` array lives in `forge-tier.yaml` alongside tool availability and tier:
    30→
    31→```yaml
    32→qmd_collections:
    33→  - name: "my-lib-extraction"
    34→    type: "extraction"
    35→    source_workflow: "create-skill"
    36→    skill_name: "my-lib"
    37→    created_at: "2026-03-14"
    38→  - name: "my-lib-brief"
    39→    type: "brief"
    40→    source_workflow: "brief-skill"
```

    44→
    45→### Collection Types
    46→
    47→| Type | Producer | Content | Primary Consumers |
    48→| --- | --- | --- | --- |
    49→| `extraction` | create-skill step-07 | Compiled SKILL.md, references, context-snippet — structured, confidence-rated exports | audit-skill (drift detection), update-skill (T2 enrichment) |
    50→| `brief` | brief-skill step-05 | skill-brief.yaml — intent, scope, target repository metadata | Portfolio-level search (cross-skill deduplication) |
    51→
    52→### Lifecycle
    53→
    54→```
    55→brief-skill writes brief → indexes {name}-brief → registers in forge-tier.yaml
    56→    ↓
    57→create-skill compiles skill → indexes {name}-extraction → registers in forge-tier.yaml
    58→    ↓
    59→audit-skill reads registry → queries {name}-extraction for temporal context
    60→update-skill reads registry → queries {name}-extraction for T2 enrichment
    61→    ↓
    62→setup-forge reads registry + QMD state → cleans orphans, removes stale entries
    63→```
    64→
    65→## Collection Gate
    66→
    67→A new QMD collection type is justified ONLY when:
    68→
    69→1. A specific **workflow step** performs a **programmatic search** against it
```
