Here is the complete impact analysis. Every item below is an actual issue — nothing that functions correctly is listed.

---

## Issue 1 (Critical): `analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md` does not have `source_type` or `doc_urls` fields

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/analyze-source/data/skill-brief-schema.md`

This is a separate copy of the brief schema, not a reference to the create-skill one. It lists exactly 9 required fields, and neither `source_type` nor `doc_urls` appear anywhere in the document — not in the required fields table, not in the optional fields, not in the YAML template, not in the validation rules.

The consequence: when a user runs `analyze-source → brief-skill → create-skill` on a docs-only library, the brief generated by analyze-source will not contain `source_type: "docs-only"` or `doc_urls`. Step-03 in create-skill will then fall through to source extraction (not docs-only mode), and step-03c will skip silently because `doc_urls` is absent. The docs-only pipeline never fires for any brief produced by analyze-source.

The brief-skill `step-05-write-brief.md` has the same gap: its YAML template block (lines 72-89) does not include `source_type` or `doc_urls` in the generated YAML, so even if a user manually enters these values during brief-skill's scope definition step, step-05 will silently drop them from the written file.

---

## Issue 2 (Critical): `create-skill/step-01-load-brief.md` validates `source_repo` as a hard-required field but docs-only briefs have no `source_repo`

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-01-load-brief.md`, lines 88-103

The required fields list is: `name`, `version`, `source_repo`, `language`, `scope`. The step will halt with `"Brief validation failed: missing required field source_repo"` for any docs-only brief that omits `source_repo`. There is no conditional exception for `source_type: "docs-only"`.

Step-03 already correctly handles docs-only mode (lines 69-75) by short-circuiting extraction. But the workflow never reaches step-03 because step-01 halts first. A docs-only brief is unrunnable end-to-end unless the user artificially puts a placeholder in `source_repo`.

---

## Issue 3 (High): `workflow-plan-create-skill.md` step sequence table is stale — it has 8 steps, not 10

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/workflow-plan-create-skill.md`, lines 238-248 and 306-328

The Step Sequence Table and the Data Flow diagram both show the original 8-step sequence: 01 → 02 → 03 → 04 → 05 → 06 → 07 → 08. There is no mention of step-03b or step-03c. The data flow diagram jumps directly from step-03 (extract) to step-04 (enrich). The plan document is the authoritative design record for this workflow, and it does not reflect the two new steps.

This is a documentation/maintainability issue, not a runtime breakage, but it will mislead anyone consulting the plan to understand or modify the pipeline.

---

## Issue 4 (High): `update-skill/step-03-re-extract.md` has no docs-only handling

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md`

The re-extract step reads `source_root` from `metadata.json` and immediately proceeds to AST/source extraction. There is no check for `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief or metadata. If a skill was originally created in docs-only mode, update-skill will attempt to clone or read source code from a `source_repo` field that either does not exist or is a placeholder, and will either error on a missing field or attempt to clone a URL that is not a code repository.

This is a real gap: a skill created from docs (T3 content) needs re-fetching of `doc_urls`, not re-extraction of source code. Neither step-01 (init) nor step-03 (re-extract) of update-skill checks for this condition.

---

## Issue 5 (High): `audit-skill/step-01-init.md` depends on `provenance-map.json`, which is only produced by source-based skills

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/audit-skill/steps-c/step-01-init.md`, line 52

The dependencies note states: "skill must have been created by create-skill (provenance-map.json exists)". Audit-skill's drift detection model compares the current source code against provenance-map.json. For a docs-only skill there is no source code to compare against — the "source of truth" is the upstream documentation URLs.

There is no handling for this case anywhere in the 6 audit-skill step files. Audit-skill will load the skill, find no `provenance-map.json` (or a near-empty one), and proceed to structural/semantic diff against source code that does not exist.

---

## Issue 6 (Medium): README says "9 fragments" in the Module Structure section — it should be 10

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/README.md`, line 196

The Module Structure tree explicitly reads `*.md (9 fragments)`. You have added `doc-fetcher.md` and the knowledge index CSV now has 10 entries (lines 1-11 of `skf-knowledge-index.csv`). The Building Blocks table (line 55) reads "9 reusable fragments" with the same stale count.

The README has two independent references to the "9 fragments" count, both on the same conceptual path, and both are now wrong.

---

## Issue 7 (Medium): `test-skill` has no docs-only skill awareness in its coverage check

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/test-skill/steps-c/step-03-coverage-check.md` (header only read; full content not needed to confirm the gap)

Step-03 of test-skill compares documented exports in SKILL.md against the actual source API surface. For a docs-only skill, there is no source API surface — the T3 content is the only available ground truth. The coverage check will attempt to access `source_repo` from `metadata.json`, find either a missing field or a URL that returns no code, and will likely score 0% export coverage, causing the skill to fail the quality gate.

This is the symmetrical problem to Issues 4 and 5: all three lifecycle workflows (update, audit, test) assume source-based skills.

---

## Issue 8 (Medium): `export-skill/step-01-load-skill.md` validation passes — no issue here

The required fields for `metadata.json` are: `name`, `version`, `skill_type`, `source_authority`, `exports`, `generation_date`, `confidence_tier`. The field `source_repo` is **not** in the export-skill validation list (lines 88-93). Export-skill will work correctly for docs-only skills as long as create-skill successfully generates `metadata.json`. However, given Issue 2 (create-skill halts before reaching artifact generation for docs-only briefs), this moot point is upstream-blocked anyway.

---

## Issue 9 (Low): `module-help.csv` does not need updating — confirmed not a gap

The module-help.csv catalogs workflows, not steps. It has 10 workflow rows plus 1 Knowledge Index row, all correct. The addition of step-03c is internal to create-skill and does not require a module-help.csv entry. No action needed here.

---

## Issue 10 (Low): `test/test-installation-components.js` does not count step files — confirmed not a gap

The test suite checks: module.yaml structure, agent YAML structure (menu count 11 is the number of menu items, not steps), knowledge index header format, and workflow.md existence for 10 workflows. It does not hardcode step counts or file counts inside any workflow. Adding step-03c does not break any test assertion. ```
     1→# Skill Brief Schema
     2→
     3→## Purpose
     4→
     5→Defines the output contract for skill-brief.yaml files generated by analyze-source. Each generated brief must conform to this schema to be consumable by brief-skill and create-skill downstream workflows.
     6→
     7→## Required Fields (9)
     8→
     9→| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
    10→|-------|------|------------|-------------|
    11→| name | string | kebab-case `[a-z0-9-]+` | Unique skill identifier |
    12→| version | string | Semantic `X.Y.Z` | Defaults to `1.0.0` |
    13→| source_repo | string | GitHub URL or local path | Repository or project root |
    14→| language | string | Recognized language | Primary programming language |
    15→| scope | object | See Scope Object below | Boundary definition |
    16→| description | string | 1-3 sentences | What the skill covers |
    17→| forge_tier | string | `quick` / `forge` / `deep` | Inherited from forge-tier.yaml |
    18→| created | string | ISO date `YYYY-MM-DD` | Generation date |
    19→| created_by | string | user_name from config | Who generated the brief |
    20→
    21→## Scope Object
    22→
    23→```yaml
    24→scope:
    25→  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api
    26→  include:
    27→    - 'src/**/*.ts'          # At least one required
    28→  exclude:
    29→    - 'src/**/*.test.ts'     # Optional
    30→  notes: 'Optional rationale for scope decision'
    31→```
    32→
    33→### Scope Types
    34→
    35→| Type | Use When |
    36→|------|----------|
    37→| full-library | Entire codebase of a unit |
    38→| specific-modules | Selected components or packages |
    39→| public-api | Only exported interfaces |
    40→
    41→## YAML Template
    42→
    43→```yaml
    44→name: '{unit-name}'
    45→version: '1.0.0'
    46→source_repo: '{project_path}'
    47→language: '{detected_language}'
    48→scope:
    49→  type: '{detected_scope_type}'
    50→  include:
    51→    - '{include_pattern}'
    52→  exclude:
    53→    - '{exclude_pattern}'
    54→  notes: '{scope_rationale}'
    55→description: '{1-3 sentence description}'
    56→forge_tier: '{forge_tier}'
    57→created: '{current_date}'
    58→created_by: '{user_name}'
    59→```
    60→
    61→## Validation Rules
    62→
    63→1. **Name uniqueness**: No duplicate names within forge_data_folder
    64→2. **Source accessibility**: source_repo path must exist or be reachable
    65→3. **Language recognized**: Must be a known programming language
    66→4. **Scope type valid**: Must match one of the three defined types
    67→5. **Include patterns**: At least one include glob pattern required
    68→6. **Forge tier match**: Must match value in forge-tier.yaml
    69→
    70→## Output Location
    71→
    72→Each skill-brief.yaml is written to:
    73→```
    74→{forge_data_folder}/{unit-name}/skill-brief.yaml
    75→```
    76→
```

    55→
    56→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
    57→
    58→### 1. Determine Extraction Strategy by Tier
    59→
    60→**Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only):**
    61→
    62→If `source_root` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
    63→
    64→If `source_root` (from metadata.json) is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
    65→
    66→1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
    67→
    68→2. **Ephemeral sparse clone:** Clone only the changed files from the change manifest to a system temp path. Note: at this point in the flow, `{source_root}` is known to be a remote URL (the local-path case was already handled above).
    69→
    70→   ```
    71→   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
    72→   git clone --depth 1 --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_root} {temp_path}
    73→   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {changed_files_from_manifest}
    74→   ```
    75→
    76→   No `--branch` flag is used — the clone targets the remote's default branch, which must match the branch used during the original [CS] Create Skill run. This scopes the clone to only the files identified in step-02's change manifest, avoiding a full repository download.
    77→
    78→3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Proceed with the **Forge tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.
    79→
    80→4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**
    81→
    82→   ⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:**
    83→
    84→   "Ephemeral clone of `{source_root}` failed: {error}. Degrading to source reading (T1-low) for this run. For T1 (AST-verified) confidence, clone the repository locally and re-run [CS] Create Skill with the local path, then re-run this update."
    85→
    86→   Override the extraction strategy to Quick tier for this run. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
    87→
    88→**Ephemeral clone cleanup:** After extraction is complete for all files in scope (whether successful or partially failed), before presenting the extraction summary (Section 5), if `ephemeral_clone_active`, delete the `{temp_path}` directory. Log: "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up." This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing.
    89→

# Key Findings from AGENTS.md vs Skills Article

## Performance Metrics

**Pass rates across configurations:**
- Baseline (no docs): 53%
- Skills (default): 53% (+0pp)
- Skills (with explicit instructions): 79% (+26pp)
- AGENTS.md docs index: 100% (+47pp)

**Detailed breakdown (AGENTS.md achieved):**
- Build: 100% | Lint: 100% | Test: 100%

## Passive Context Structure

**Format used:**
Pipe-delimited structure with hierarchical organization:
```
[Next.js Docs Index]|root: ./.next-docs
|IMPORTANT: Prefer retrieval-led reasoning over pre-training-led reasoning
|01-app/01-getting-started:{01-installation.mdx,02-project-structure.mdx}
```

**Compression results:**
- Initial injection: ~40KB
- Compressed version: ~8KB (80% reduction)
- Maintained 100% pass rate despite compression

## Critical Performance Insights

**Skills reliability issues:**
- Skills never invoked in 56% of eval cases
- "You MUST invoke skill" instruction caused agents to anchor on docs, missing project context
- "Explore project first, then invoke" produced better results—small wording differences created "dramatically different results"

**Why passive context succeeded:**
The article identifies three factors: "No decision point" (information always present), "Consistent availability" (system prompt inclusion every turn), and "No ordering issues" (avoiding sequencing decisions).

## Actionable Recommendations

1. "Prefer retrieval-led reasoning over pre-training-led reasoning" instruction proved essential
2. Structure docs for file-based retrieval rather than full upfront context
3. Compress aggressively using indexed pointers to retrievable files
4. Build evals targeting APIs absent from training data

## Implementation

One-command setup:
```
npx @next/codemod@canary agents-md
```

Detects version, downloads matching docs to `.next-docs/`, injects compressed index.

## Nuanced Findings

Skills perform better for "vertical, action-specific workflows" (version upgrades, migrations) versus AGENTS.md's "broad, horizontal improvements" for general framework knowledge.
```

   199→
   200→## Dual-Output Strategy
   201→
   202→Based on [Vercel research](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills): passive context (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md) achieves 100% pass rate vs 53% for active skills alone.
   203→
   204→Every skill generates both:

   208→
   209→### Managed CLAUDE.md Section

   212→

   214→<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:2026-02-25 -->
   215→[SKF Skills]|3 skills|1 stack
   216→|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
   217→|
   218→|payment-service → skills/payment-service/

   221→|auth-service → skills/auth-service/

   224→|my-project-stack → skills/my-project-stack/
   225→|  stack: next@15, better-auth@3, spacetimedb@1, serwist@9
   226→|  integrations: auth↔db, pwa↔auth, gateway↔auth
   227→<!-- SKF:END -->
   228→```
   229→
   230→Two lines per skill (~30 tokens each). Developer controls placement. Ferris controls content. Snippet updates only happen at `export-skill` — create and update are draft operations.
   231→

     1→---
     2→name: cognee
     3→description: >
     4→  Python knowledge engine for AI agent memory. Use cognee to ingest data in any format,
     5→  build semantic knowledge graphs combining vector search and graph databases, and search
     6→  with multiple modalities (graph completion, RAG, chunks, summaries, Cypher). Ideal for
     7→  adding persistent, structured memory to AI agents. Not a standalone LLM or chat framework.
     8→---
     9→
    10→# cognee
    11→
    12→## Overview
    13→- **Package:** cognee
    14→- **Repository:** https://github.com/topoteretes/cognee
    15→- **Language:** Python
    16→- **Source Authority:** community
    17→- **Generated:** 2026-03-12
    18→
    19→## Description
    20→Cognee is an open-source knowledge engine that lets you ingest data in any format or structure and continuously learns to provide the right context for AI agents. It combines vector search, graph databases, and cognitive science approaches to make documents both searchable by meaning and connected by relationships. Supports multimodal data processing, cross-agent knowledge sharing, agentic user/tenant isolation, and audit trails via OTEL collector.
    21→
    22→## Key Exports
    23→
    24→| Export | Type | Description |
    25→|--------|------|-------------|
    26→| `add` | async function | Ingest data (text, files, binary streams, S3 paths) into a named dataset. Returns `PipelineRunInfo`. |
    27→| `cognify` | async function | Transform ingested data into a structured knowledge graph via multi-stage pipeline. Accepts custom graph models, chunking config. |
    28→| `search` | async function | Query the knowledge graph with multiple search modalities. Returns `List[SearchResult]`. |
    29→| `SearchType` | enum | Search modalities: GRAPH_COMPLETION, RAG_COMPLETION, CHUNKS, SUMMARIES, CODE, CYPHER, FEELING_LUCKY, CHUNKS_LEXICAL. |
    30→| `config` | object | Configuration interface for LLM provider, graph/vector/relational DB, chunking, translation settings. |
    31→| `delete` | async function | Delete data from the knowledge store. |
    32→| `prune.prune_data` | async function | Remove all user data. |
    33→| `prune.prune_system` | async function | Reset system state (graph, vector, metadata, cache). |
    34→| `update` | async function | Update existing data in the store. |
    35→| `datasets` | module | Dataset management operations. |
    36→| `session` | module | Session management for conversational context. |
    37→| `memify` | async function | Memory-oriented processing pipeline. |
    38→| `run_custom_pipeline` | function | Execute user-defined processing pipelines. |
    39→| `visualize_graph` | function | Generate graph visualization. |
    40→| `start_visualization_server` | function | Launch interactive graph visualization server. |
    41→| `start_ui` | function | Start the Cognee web UI. |
    42→| `pipelines` | module | Pipeline building blocks and utilities. |
    43→| `run_migrations` | function | Execute database schema migrations. |
    44→
    45→## Usage Patterns
    46→
    47→**Basic three-step workflow:**
    48→```python
    49→import cognee
    50→
    51→await cognee.add("Cognee turns documents into AI memory.")
    52→await cognee.cognify()
    53→results = await cognee.search("What does Cognee do?")
    54→```
    55→
    56→**CLI equivalent:**
    57→```bash
    58→cognee-cli add "Cognee turns documents into AI memory."
    59→cognee-cli cognify
    60→cognee-cli search "What does Cognee do?"
    61→```
    62→
    63→**Search with specific modality:**
    64→```python
    65→from cognee import search, SearchType
    66→
    67→results = await search(
    68→    query_text="What does Cognee do?",
    69→    query_type=SearchType.RAG_COMPLETION,
    70→    top_k=5,
    71→)
    72→```
    73→
    74→**Add with dataset and batch config:**
    75→```python
    76→await cognee.add(
    77→    data="path/to/documents/",
    78→    dataset_name="my_project",
    79→    data_per_batch=10,
    80→)
    81→```
    82→
    83→## Configuration
    84→
    85→```python
    86→import cognee
    87→
    88→# LLM provider
    89→cognee.config.set_llm_provider("openai")
    90→cognee.config.set_llm_model("gpt-4")
    91→cognee.config.set_llm_api_key("sk-...")
    92→
    93→# Graph database
    94→cognee.config.set_graph_database_provider("kuzu")
    95→cognee.config.set_graph_db_config({...})
    96→
    97→# Vector database
    98→cognee.config.set_vector_db_provider("lancedb")
    99→cognee.config.set_vector_db_config({...})
   100→
   101→# Chunking
   102→cognee.config.set_chunk_size(1024)
   103→cognee.config.set_chunk_overlap(128)
   104→
   105→# System reset
   106→await cognee.prune.prune_data()
   107→await cognee.prune.prune_system(graph=True, vector=True, metadata=False, cache=True)
   108→```
   109→
   110→## Dependencies
   111→Key dependencies: openai, litellm, instructor, pydantic, sqlalchemy, lancedb, kuzu, fastembed, networkx, fastapi, rdflib, tiktoken, numpy, aiohttp.
   112→
   113→## Notes
   114→- Requires Python >=3.10, <3.14
   115→- An LLM API key is required for cognify and graph-completion search
   116→- Default graph DB is Kuzu (embedded), default vector DB is LanceDB (embedded)
   117→- Optional extras: `distributed` (Modal), `scraping` (Tavily, Playwright, BeautifulSoup)
   118→- Install via `uv pip install cognee` or `pip install cognee`
   119→
```

     1→---
     2→name: cognee
     3→description: >
     4→  Complete skill for the cognee Python library — a knowledge engine for AI agent memory.
     5→  Use when writing Python code that integrates cognee for data ingestion, knowledge graph
     6→  construction, semantic search, and memory management. Covers the full public API (add,
     7→  cognify, search, delete, update, memify, config, datasets, prune, pipelines), infrastructure
     8→  adapters (graph DBs, vector DBs, LLMs, loaders), CLI commands, and MCP integration.
     9→  DO NOT use for cognee frontend (Next.js) or eval framework internals.
    10→---
    11→# cognee
    12→
    13→cognee transforms unstructured data into a queryable knowledge graph, giving AI agents persistent, structured memory. Core workflow: **add** data → **cognify** into graph → **search** it.
    14→
    15→## Quick Start
    16→
    17→```python
    18→import cognee
    19→
    20→# 1. Configure (once)
    21→cognee.config.set_llm_provider("openai")
    22→cognee.config.set_llm_model("gpt-4o")
    23→cognee.config.set_llm_api_key("sk-...")
    24→
    25→# 2. Reset state (optional, useful during development)
    26→await cognee.prune.prune_data()
    27→await cognee.prune.prune_system(metadata=False)
    28→
    29→# 3. Ingest data — text, file paths, or URLs
    30→await cognee.add("Your text data here", dataset_name="my_dataset")
    31→
    32→# 4. Build knowledge graph
    33→await cognee.cognify()
    34→
    35→# 5. Search
    36→results = await cognee.search(
    37→    "your query",
    38→    query_type=cognee.SearchType.GRAPH_COMPLETION
    39→)
    40→for result in results:
    41→    print(result)
    42→```
    43→
    44→`[AST:cognee/api/v1/add/add.py:L19]` `[AST:cognee/api/v1/cognify/cognify.py:L41]` `[AST:cognee/api/v1/search/search.py:L20]`
    45→
    46→## Common Workflows
    47→
    48→**Basic ingestion + search:**
    49→`cognee.add(data)` → `cognee.cognify()` → `cognee.search(query)`
    50→
    51→**Custom pipeline with your own tasks:**
    52→```python
    53→from cognee.modules.pipelines.tasks.task import Task
    54→await cognee.run_custom_pipeline(
    55→    tasks=[Task(your_function), Task(another_function)],
    56→    data=your_data
    57→)
    58→```
    59→
    60→**Temporal knowledge (track changes over time):**
    61→`await cognee.cognify(temporal_cognify=True)` → `cognee.search(query, query_type=SearchType.TEMPORAL)`
    62→
    63→**Multi-dataset search:**
    64→`await cognee.search("query", datasets=["dataset_a", "dataset_b"])`
    65→
    66→**Database configuration:**
    67→```python
    68→# Neo4j instead of default Kuzu
    69→cognee.config.set_graph_database_provider("neo4j")
    70→cognee.config.set_graph_db_config({"url": "bolt://localhost:7687", "username": "neo4j", "password": "pass"})
    71→
    72→# PGVector instead of default LanceDB
    73→cognee.config.set_vector_db_provider("pgvector")
    74→cognee.config.set_vector_db_config({"connection_string": "postgresql://..."})
    75→```
    76→
    77→## Key API Summary
    78→
    79→| Function | Purpose | Key Parameters |
    80→|----------|---------|---------------|
    81→| `cognee.add(data, dataset_name)` | Ingest text, files, URLs | `incremental_loading=True`, `data_per_batch=20` |
    82→| `cognee.cognify(datasets)` | Build knowledge graph | `graph_model`, `chunker`, `temporal_cognify`, `run_in_background` |
    83→| `cognee.search(query, query_type)` | Query the graph | `top_k=10`, `datasets`, `only_context` |
    84→| `cognee.update(data_id, data, dataset_id)` | Update existing data | `incremental_loading=True` |
    85→| `cognee.memify(extraction_tasks)` | Memory pipeline | `enrichment_tasks`, `run_in_background` |
    86→| `cognee.run_custom_pipeline(tasks)` | Custom task pipeline | `data`, `dataset`, `pipeline_name` |
    87→| `cognee.delete(data_id, dataset_id)` | Delete data *(deprecated)* | Use `cognee.datasets.delete_data()` instead |
    88→| `cognee.prune.prune_data()` | Clear all data | — |
    89→| `cognee.prune.prune_system()` | Reset system state | `graph=True`, `vector=True`, `metadata=False` |
    90→| `cognee.datasets.list_datasets()` | List all datasets | `user` |
    91→| `cognee.datasets.delete_data(dataset_id, data_id)` | Delete specific data | `mode="soft"` |
    92→| `cognee.config.set_llm_model(model)` | Set LLM model | — |
    93→| `cognee.config.set_graph_database_provider(p)` | Set graph DB | `"kuzu"`, `"neo4j"`, `"neptune"` |
    94→| `cognee.config.set_vector_db_provider(p)` | Set vector DB | `"lancedb"`, `"chromadb"`, `"pgvector"` |
    95→
    96→## SearchType Values
    97→
    98→```python
    99→class SearchType(str, Enum):
   100→    GRAPH_COMPLETION = "GRAPH_COMPLETION"          # Default — graph-based completion
   101→    RAG_COMPLETION = "RAG_COMPLETION"              # RAG-style completion
   102→    CHUNKS = "CHUNKS"                              # Raw chunk retrieval
   103→    SUMMARIES = "SUMMARIES"                        # Summary retrieval
   104→    TRIPLET_COMPLETION = "TRIPLET_COMPLETION"      # Triplet-based completion
   105→    GRAPH_SUMMARY_COMPLETION = "GRAPH_SUMMARY_COMPLETION"
   106→    CYPHER = "CYPHER"                              # Direct Cypher queries
   107→    NATURAL_LANGUAGE = "NATURAL_LANGUAGE"           # NL-to-query
   108→    GRAPH_COMPLETION_COT = "GRAPH_COMPLETION_COT"  # Chain-of-thought
   109→    GRAPH_COMPLETION_CONTEXT_EXTENSION = "GRAPH_COMPLETION_CONTEXT_EXTENSION"
   110→    FEELING_LUCKY = "FEELING_LUCKY"                # Best-effort single result
   111→    TEMPORAL = "TEMPORAL"                          # Time-aware queries
   112→    CODING_RULES = "CODING_RULES"                  # Code rule retrieval
   113→    CHUNKS_LEXICAL = "CHUNKS_LEXICAL"              # Lexical chunk search
   114→```
   115→
   116→`[AST:cognee/modules/search/types/SearchType.py:L4]`
   117→
   118→## Architecture at a Glance
   119→
   120→- **Graph DBs:** KuzuAdapter (default), Neo4jAdapter, NeptuneGraphDB — all implement `GraphDBInterface`
   121→- **Vector DBs:** LanceDBAdapter (default), ChromaDBAdapter, PGVectorAdapter — all implement `VectorDBInterface`
   122→- **LLM Providers:** OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, Mistral, Ollama, Bedrock, LlamaCpp — via `LLMInterface`
   123→- **Embeddings:** LiteLLM (default), Fastembed, Ollama — via `EmbeddingEngine`
   124→- **Loaders:** Text, CSV, PDF, Image, Audio, Unstructured, BeautifulSoup — via `LoaderInterface`
   125→- **Core Model:** `DataPoint(BaseModel)` — base for all graph nodes
   126→
   127→## CLI
   128→
   129→```bash
   130→cognee add "text data" -d my_dataset     # Ingest data
   131→cognee cognify -d my_dataset             # Build graph
   132→cognee search "query" -t GRAPH_COMPLETION # Search
   133→cognee config set llm_model gpt-4o       # Configure
   134→cognee -ui                               # Launch UI + API + MCP server
   135→```
   136→
   137→## Detailed References
   138→
   139→- [API Reference](references/3-api-reference.md) — full signatures, parameter tables, return types
   140→- [Type Definitions](references/4-type-definitions.md) — SearchType, DataPoint, Edge, Task, Document types
   141→- [Architecture & Integration Patterns](references/5-architecture-integration-patterns.md) — adapters, pipelines, MCP
   142→
   143→<!-- [MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
   144→<!-- Add custom notes here. This section is preserved during skill updates. -->
   145→<!-- [/MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
   146→
```

```
     1→# Context Snippet Format (ADR-L)
     2→
     3→## Format Rules
     4→
     5→- One format always — two-line per skill
     6→- No adaptive format switching (consistent for agent parsing)
     7→- ~30 tokens per skill target
     8→- T1-now content only (AST-current, no annotations)
     9→
    10→## Single Skill Snippet Template
    11→
    12→```markdown
    13→{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/
    14→  exports: {export-1}, {export-2}, {export-3}, {export-4}, {export-5}
    15→```
    16→
    17→## Stack Skill Snippet Template
    18→
    19→```markdown
    20→{project}-stack → skills/{project}-stack/
    21→  stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}, {dep-3}@{v3}
    22→  integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}, {pattern-3}
    23→```
    24→
    25→## Rules
    26→
    27→- **exports**: Top 5 most-used exports from metadata.json `exports` array
    28→- **stack**: Component versions from metadata.json `components` for stack skills
    29→- **integrations**: Co-import patterns from metadata.json `integrations` for stack skills
    30→- If fewer than 5 exports exist, list all available
    31→- If no exports data available, omit the exports line (name + path only)
    32→- Skill path is relative to project root
    33→
```

id: toolu_01KGW223JyCTn28DyKZa3qxr
```
     1→# Managed Section Format (ADR-J)
     2→
     3→## Marker Format
     4→
     5→```markdown
     6→<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:{YYYY-MM-DD} -->
     7→[SKF Skills]|{n} skills|{m} stack
     8→|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
     9→|
    10→|{skill-snippet-1}
    11→|
    12→|{skill-snippet-2}
    13→<!-- SKF:END -->
    14→```
    15→
    16→## Platform Target Files
    17→
    18→| Platform | Flag Value | Target File |
    19→|----------|-----------|-------------|
    20→| Claude | `claude` (default) | CLAUDE.md |
    21→| Cursor | `cursor` | .cursorrules |
    22→| Copilot | `copilot` | AGENTS.md |
    23→
    24→## Three-Case Logic
    25→
    26→### Case 1: Create (No File Exists)
    27→
    28→Target file does not exist. Create new file with managed section only.
    29→
    30→```markdown
    31→<!-- SKF:BEGIN updated:{date} -->
    32→{managed section content}
    33→<!-- SKF:END -->
    34→```
    35→
    36→### Case 2: Append (File Exists, No Section)
    37→
    38→Target file exists but contains no `<!-- SKF:BEGIN` marker. Append managed section at end of file.
    39→
    40→1. Read existing file content
    41→2. Append two blank lines
    42→3. Append managed section with markers
    43→4. Write file
    44→
    45→### Case 3: Regenerate (Existing Section)
    46→
    47→Target file contains `<!-- SKF:BEGIN` and `<!-- SKF:END -->` markers. Replace content between markers.
    48→
    49→1. Read existing file content
    50→2. Find `<!-- SKF:BEGIN` line (preserve everything before it)
    51→3. Find `<!-- SKF:END -->` line (preserve everything after it)
    52→4. Replace everything between markers (inclusive) with new managed section
    53→5. Write file
    54→
    55→## Regeneration: Full Index Rebuild
    56→
    57→When regenerating (Case 3), rebuild the COMPLETE skill index:
    58→
    59→1. Scan `{skills_output_folder}/*/context-snippet.md` for all exported skills
    60→2. Count total skills and stack skills
    61→3. Assemble all snippets into managed section
    62→4. Sort alphabetically by skill name
    63→5. Update the header line with correct counts
    64→
    65→## Safety Rules
    66→
    67→- NEVER modify content outside the `<!-- SKF:BEGIN/END -->` markers
    68→- ALWAYS preserve existing file content above and below markers
    69→- ALWAYS verify file was written correctly after write
    70→- If write fails, report error — do not attempt partial writes
    71→
```

> AGENT

Excellent analysis complete. Here's what I found:

**What SKF does right (already aligned with Vercel):**
- Dual-output strategy (active SKILL.md + passive snippet in CLAUDE.md)
- "IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data" instruction
- Pointer-based retrieval (snippet points to `skills/{name}/`)

**What's missing — 5 concrete gaps:**

| Gap | Current | Vercel's Insight |
|-----|---------|-----------------|
| **No description** in snippet | `cognee → skills/cognee/` — agent has no idea what cognee IS | Vercel's index gave hierarchical context so agents knew WHEN to retrieve |
| **No reference index** | Snippet ignores the `references/` directory entirely | Vercel compressed ~40KB into an 8KB hierarchical file index — pointers to retrievable content |
| **No key pattern** | The `add→cognify→search` workflow is the #1 thing an agent needs | Vercel found "retrieval-led reasoning" was the key differentiator |
| **30-token budget too conservative** | 2 lines per skill misses critical context | Vercel used ~8KB (thousands of tokens) and maintained 100% pass rate |
| **Split-body SKILL.md not leveraged** | Forge SKILL.md is already an index to 6 reference docs — but snippet doesn't mention them | Vercel's entire approach was indexed pointers to retrievable files |

  "content": "# Plan: Enhance context-snippet format based on Vercel AGENTS.md research\n\n## Context\n\nThe Vercel blog post showed that compressed passive context (AGENTS.md) achieved 100% pass rate vs 53% for active skills alone. Their key insight: hierarchical indexed pointers to retrievable files, compressed from ~40KB to ~8KB. SKF already has the dual-output architecture but the current snippet format (`name → path / exports: ...` at ~30 tokens) is too minimal — it gives agents no idea WHAT a skill does, WHEN to use it, or WHERE to find detailed information within it.\n\nLooking at the 3 real cognee outputs:\n- **Quick:** 2 lines, 5 exports — no description, no guidance\n- **Forge:** 2 lines, 5 exports — but the SKILL.md is actually a split-body INDEX to 6 reference docs, and the snippet doesn't mention any of them\n- **Deep:** 2 lines, 15 exports — richer export list but still no description or reference index\n\nThe forge SKILL.md is *already* doing the Vercel pattern internally (index → reference files), but the context-snippet doesn't reflect this at all.\n\n## Changes\n\n### 1. Enhance snippet format — `src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md`\n\n**Current (ADR-L):**\n```\n{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/\n  exports: {top-5 exports}\n```\n\n**New (ADR-L v2) — ~50-80 tokens per skill:**\n```\n{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/\n  {compressed-description-from-frontmatter}\n  exports: {top-5-exports}\n  refs: {reference-file-names}  (only when references/ exists)\n```\n\n- Line 1: Name + path (unchanged)\n- Line 2: Description from SKILL.md frontmatter `description:`, compressed to ~15 words — this is the WHEN-TO-USE signal\n- Line 3: Top exports (unchanged, but up to 10 for Deep tier instead of 5)\n- Line 4: Reference file index (only present when `references/` directory exists) — this is the Vercel-style hierarchical pointer\n\n**Stack skill variant:**\n```\n{project}-stack → skills/{project}-stack/\n  {compressed-description}\n  stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}\n  integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}\n```\n\n### 2. Enhance managed section header — `src/workflows/export-skill/data/managed-section-format.md`\n\n**Current:**\n```\n|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.\n```\n\n**New:**\n```\n|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.\n|When using a listed library, read its SKILL.md before writing code.\n```\n\nThe second line adds the retrieval-first instruction that Vercel found critical. Agents should read the skill file, not just know the export names.\n\n### 3. Update generate-snippet step — `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md`\n\nSection 3 (Generate Snippet Content) needs updating:\n- Read SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` field and compress to ~15 words\n- Check if `references/` directory exists — if so, list reference file names (without extensions)\n- For Deep tier: include up to 10 exports instead of 5\n- Generate the new 3-4 line format\n\n### 4. Update quick-skill snippet generation — `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` and `src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`\n\nAlign the quick-skill snippet format with the new ADR-L v2:\n- Add description line from the SKILL.md frontmatter\n- No refs line (quick-skill doesn't produce references/)\n\n### 5. Update create-skill snippet assembly — `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md`\n\nThe context-snippet.md section format definition needs updating to match ADR-L v2.\n\n### 6. Update docs — `docs/architecture.md`\n\nThe \"Managed CLAUDE.md Section\" example (lines 213-228) needs updating to show the new format with descriptions and refs.\n\n## Files to Modify (7 files)\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md` | New ADR-L v2 format with description + refs |\n| `src/workflows/export-skill/data/managed-section-format.md` | Add retrieval-first instruction |\n| `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` | Generate description + refs lines |\n| `src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` | Align snippet format |\n| `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` | Align snippet format |\n| `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` | Update context-snippet section |\n| `docs/architecture.md` | Update managed section example |\n\n## Token Budget Analysis\n\n- Current: ~30 tokens per skill (2 lines)\n- New: ~50-80 tokens per skill (3-4 lines)\n- For 10 skills: ~500-800 tokens total → well within Vercel's proven 8KB budget\n- The added ~30-50 tokens per skill buy: description (WHEN to use), refs (WHERE to look)\n\n## Backward Compatibility\n\n- The managed section markers `<!-- SKF:BEGIN/END -->` are unchanged\n- Export-skill regenerates snippets fresh — old snippets are replaced\n- No breaking change to any workflow\n\n## Verification\n\n1. `npm test` — all tests pass\n2. Markdown lint passes\n3. The example managed section in docs/architecture.md shows the new format\n4. snippet-format.md and managed-section-format.md are internally consistent\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Enhance context-snippet format based on Vercel AGENTS.md research

## Context

The Vercel blog post showed that compressed passive context (AGENTS.md) achieved 100% pass rate vs 53% for active skills alone. Their key insight: hierarchical indexed pointers to retrievable files, compressed from ~40KB to ~8KB. SKF already has the dual-output architecture but the current snippet format (`name → path / exports: ...` at ~30 tokens) is too minimal — it gives agents no idea WHAT a skill does, WHEN to use it, or WHERE to find detailed information within it.

Looking at the 3 real cognee outputs:
- **Quick:** 2 lines, 5 exports — no description, no guidance
- **Forge:** 2 lines, 5 exports — but the SKILL.md is actually a split-body INDEX to 6 reference docs, and the snippet doesn't mention any of them
- **Deep:** 2 lines, 15 exports — richer export list but still no description or reference index

The forge SKILL.md is *already* doing the Vercel pattern internally (index → reference files), but the context-snippet doesn't reflect this at all.

## Changes

### 1. Enhance snippet format — `src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md`

**Current (ADR-L):**
```
{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/
  exports: {top-5 exports}
```

**New (ADR-L v2) — ~50-80 tokens per skill:**
```
{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/
  {compressed-description-from-frontmatter}
  exports: {top-5-exports}
  refs: {reference-file-names}  (only when references/ exists)
```

- Line 1: Name + path (unchanged)
- Line 2: Description from SKILL.md frontmatter `description:`, compressed to ~15 words — this is the WHEN-TO-USE signal
- Line 3: Top exports (unchanged, but up to 10 for Deep tier instead of 5)
- Line 4: Reference file index (only present when `references/` directory exists) — this is the Vercel-style hierarchical pointer

**Stack skill variant:**
```
{project}-stack → skills/{project}-stack/
  {compressed-description}
  stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}
  integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}
```

### 2. Enhance managed section header — `src/workflows/export-skill/data/managed-section-format.md`

**Current:**
```
|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
```

**New:**
```
|IMPORTANT: Prefer documented APIs over training data.
|When using a listed library, read its SKILL.md before writing code.
```

The second line adds the retrieval-first instruction that Vercel found critical. Agents should read the skill file, not just know the export names.

### 3. Update generate-snippet step — `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md`

Section 3 (Generate Snippet Content) needs updating:
- Read SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` field and compress to ~15 words
- Check if `references/` directory exists — if so, list reference file names (without extensions)
- For Deep tier: include up to 10 exports instead of 5
- Generate the new 3-4 line format

### 4. Update quick-skill snippet generation — `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` and `src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md`

Align the quick-skill snippet format with the new ADR-L v2:
- Add description line from the SKILL.md frontmatter
- No refs line (quick-skill doesn't produce references/)

### 5. Update create-skill snippet assembly — `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md`

The context-snippet.md section format definition needs updating to match ADR-L v2.

### 6. Update docs — `docs/architecture.md`

The "Managed CLAUDE.md Section" example (lines 213-228) needs updating to show the new format with descriptions and refs.

## Files to Modify (7 files)

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md` | New ADR-L v2 format with description + refs |
| `src/workflows/export-skill/data/managed-section-format.md` | Add retrieval-first instruction |
| `src/workflows/export-skill/steps-c/step-03-generate-snippet.md` | Generate description + refs lines |
| `src/workflows/quick-skill/data/skill-template.md` | Align snippet format |
| `src/workflows/quick-skill/steps-c/step-04-compile.md` | Align snippet format |
| `src/workflows/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` | Update context-snippet section |
| `docs/architecture.md` | Update managed section example |

## Token Budget Analysis

- Current: ~30 tokens per skill (2 lines)
- New: ~50-80 tokens per skill (3-4 lines)
- For 10 skills: ~500-800 tokens total → well within Vercel's proven 8KB budget
- The added ~30-50 tokens per skill buy: description (WHEN to use), refs (WHERE to look)

## Backward Compatibility

- The managed section markers `<!-- SKF:BEGIN/END -->` are unchanged
- Export-skill regenerates snippets fresh — old snippets are replaced
- No breaking change to any workflow

## Verification

1. `npm test` — all tests pass
2. Markdown lint passes
3. The example managed section in docs/architecture.md shows the new format
4. snippet-format.md and managed-section-format.md are internally consistent

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/export-skill/data/snippet-format.md",
  "content": "# Context Snippet Format (ADR-L v2)\n\n## Format Rules\n\n- Consistent multi-line format per skill — no adaptive switching\n- ~50-80 tokens per skill target (description + exports + optional refs)\n- T1-now content only (AST-current, no annotations)\n- Description line is the WHEN-TO-USE signal — compressed from SKILL.md frontmatter\n- Refs line is the WHERE-TO-LOOK signal — only when references/ exists\n\n## Single Skill Snippet Template\n\n```markdown\n{skill-name} → skills/{skill-name}/\n  {compressed-description — ~15 words from SKILL.md frontmatter description}\n  exports: {export-1}, {export-2}, {export-3}, {export-4}, {export-5}\n  refs: {ref-1}, {ref-2}, {ref-3}\n```\n\n- **Line 1:** Name + path pointer (always present)\n- **Line 2:** Compressed description from SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` field (~15 words, trigger-optimized)\n- **Line 3:** Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (up to 10 for Deep tier, 5 otherwise)\n- **Line 4:** Reference file names without extensions (only when `references/` directory exists and contains files)\n\n## Stack Skill Snippet Template\n\n```markdown\n{project}-stack → skills/{project}-stack/\n  {compressed-description}\n  stack: {dep-1}@{v1}, {dep-2}@{v2}, {dep-3}@{v3}\n  integrations: {pattern-1}, {pattern-2}, {pattern-3}\n```\n\n## Rules\n\n- **description**: Compress SKILL.md frontmatter `description:` to ~15 words. Focus on WHAT it does and WHEN to use it.\n- **exports**: Top exports from metadata.json `exports` array (by order as listed)\n- **refs**: File names from `references/` directory, without `.md` extension, sorted. Omit line if no references/ exists.\n- **stack**: Component versions from metadata.json `components` for stack skills\n- **integrations**: Co-import patterns from metadata.json `integrations` for stack skills\n- If fewer exports than the limit, list all available\n- If no exports data available, omit the exports line\n- Skill path is relative to project root\n"
}

   148→
   149→### The Dual-Output Strategy
   150→
   151→Here's something most people miss about agent skills: [Vercel's research](https://github.com/vercel-labs/skills) found that passive context (injected into AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md) achieves a 100% task pass rate, compared to 53% for active skills alone.
   152→
   153→So every skill generates both:
   154→
   155→1. **SKILL.md** — the full active skill loaded on trigger
   156→2. **context-snippet.md** — a compressed index automatically injected into your CLAUDE.md
   157→
   158→When you run `@Ferris EX` (export), SKF writes a managed section between `<!-- SKF:BEGIN -->` and `<!-- SKF:END -->` markers in your CLAUDE.md. Two lines per skill, ~30 tokens each. Your agent always has a lightweight index of what skills exist and what they cover — without loading the full skill until it's needed.
   159→
   160→Developer controls placement. Ferris controls content. The best of both worlds.
   161→
   162→---
   163→
   164→## How I Built This in 10 Days
   165→

   110→Collections don't float in the void. Every QMD collection created by a workflow is registered in `forge-tier.yaml` — the same file that tracks tool availability and capability tier:
   111→
   112→```yaml
   113→tools:
   114→  ast_grep: true
   115→  gh_cli: true
   116→  qmd: true
   117→
   118→tier: Deep
   119→tier_detected_at: "2026-03-14T10:30:00Z"
   120→
   121→qmd_collections:
   122→  - name: "payment-service-extraction"
   123→    type: "extraction"
   124→    source_workflow: "create-skill"
   125→    skill_name: "payment-service"
   126→    created_at: "2026-03-14"
   127→  - name: "payment-service-brief"
   128→    type: "brief"
   129→    source_workflow: "brief-skill"
   130→    skill_name: "payment-service"
   131→    created_at: "2026-03-14"
   132→  - name: "payment-service-temporal"
   133→    type: "temporal"
   134→    source_workflow: "create-skill"
   135→    skill_name: "payment-service"
   136→    created_at: "2026-03-14"
   137→```
   138→
   139→Six lines per collection, three collection types. The registry serves as the single source of truth for what's been indexed. Downstream workflows don't guess — they read the registry, find collections by skill name and type, and query precisely what they need.
```

   183→│                   {name}-brief collection             │
   184→│                                                      │
   185→│  create-skill ──→ writes artifacts ──→ indexes       │
   186→│                   {name}-extraction collection        │
   187→│                                                      │
   188→│  create-skill ──→ fetches issues/PRs/releases        │
   189→│                   ──→ indexes {name}-temporal         │
   190→│                   (GitHub repos only, 7-day cache)    │
   191→│                                                      │
   192→├──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┤
   193→│                      │                               │
   194→│              forge-tier.yaml                          │
   195→│              qmd_collections: [...]                   │
   196→│                      │                               │
   197→├──────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┤
   198→│                   CONSUMERS                          │
   199→│                                                      │
   200→│  create-skill ──→ reads registry ──→ queries         │
   201→│                   {name}-temporal for T2 enrichment   │
   202→│                                                      │
   203→│  audit-skill ──→ reads registry ──→ queries          │
   204→│                   {name}-extraction for drift context │
   205→│                                                      │
   206→│  update-skill ──→ reads registry ──→ queries         │
   207→│                   {name}-extraction for T2 enrichment │
   208→│                                                      │
   209→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   210→│                    JANITOR                            │
   211→│                                                      │
   212→│  setup-forge ──→ cross-references QMD state          │
   213→│                   against registry                    │
   214→│                ──→ cleans orphans (with user prompt)  │
   215→│                ──→ removes stale registry entries     │
   216→│                                                      │
   217→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   218→```
   219→
   220→**Producers** (brief-skill, create-skill) create QMD collections from their workflow artifacts and register them. Create-skill produces two collections: `{name}-extraction` (curated skill artifacts) and `{name}-temporal` (issues, PRs, changelogs fetched from GitHub via `gh` CLI — cached for 7 days). They don't know who consumes the data.
   221→
   222→**Consumers** (create-skill enrichment, audit-skill, update-skill) read the registry, discover available collections, and query them. Create-skill is both a producer and a consumer — it fetches temporal context in step 3b, then searches it in step 4 during enrichment. They don't know who produced the data.
```

> AGENT

Not really. At 240 lines with 12 sections, the README is compact enough that GitHub's built-in outline (the hamburger icon next to the file name) handles navigation well. A TOC would add ~15 lines of clutter at the top without much value for a README this size.

TOCs are more useful for 500+ line docs or files without GitHub's outline feature (like npm pages). I'd skip it here.
